      "title": "Restaurants & Dining | Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel",
      "content": "## Kyo Kyo Vegetarian Restaurant\n\nVegetarian\n\nWhether you are a vegetarian by choice or simply looking to discover something different, Kyo Kyo, this downtown Kaohsiung vegetarian restaurant, invites you to experience how plant-based cuisine can be vibrant, innovative, and deeply satisfying.\n\nM-Chef Steak & Teppanyaki\n\n## M-CHEF TEPPANYAKI\n\nJapanese\n\nM Chef Teppanyaki showcases premium seasonal ingredients, masterful teppan craftsmanship, and live culinary artistry. Enjoy an immersive dining experience where every dish is prepared to perfection.\n\nMajesty Restaurant\n\n## Majesty Steak House\n\nSteakhouse [...] Majesty Restaurant\n\n## Majesty Steak House\n\nSteakhouse\n\nAt Majesty Steak House, we give the classic steakhouse a modern twist. This Kaohsiung city center restaurant uses fresh, top-quality ingredients and bold flavors. Enjoy juicy steaks and creative bistro dishes where tradition meets innovation.\n\nT-Fusion\n\n## T-Fusion\n\nCoffee House\n\nBy day, savor specialty coffee and comforting café favorites; by night, unwind with handcrafted cocktails and live jazz. T-FUSION offers an effortlessly elegant day-to-night experience high above the city.\n\nM Bread & Pastry\n\n## M Bread ＆ Pastry\n\nOther [...] ## Huang Hao Chinese Restaurant\n\nChinese\n\nWhether combo meals or prix fixe, Huang Hao Chinese Restaurant, downtown Kaohsiung restaurants​ near Love River, offers an authentic cuisine experience. Savor a wide variety of dishes, including the must-try roast duck.\n\nKyo Raku Japanese Cuisine & Hot Pot\n\n## Kyo Raku Japanese Cuisine\n\nJapanese\n\nDiscover the elegance of Japanese at Kyo Raku, led by Chef Masanori Watanabe. Rooted in traditional craftsmanship and seasonal ingredients, we offers exquisite Kaiseki and beautifully presented creations that deliver a memorable culinary experience.\n\nThe Lounge\n\n## The Lounge\n\nMultiple cuisines",

      "raw_content": "![Marriott Hotels and Resorts](https://cache.marriott.com/content/dam/marriott-digital/mc/global-property-shared/en_us/logo/assets/mc_logo_L.png)\n\n## Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel\n\n### Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel\n\n![Majesty Restaurant](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n![Credit Card](/content/dam/marriott-digital/digital-merchandising/us-canada/en_us/cobrand-cards/assets/pdt-0513-DCA-125892172130576.png)\n\n# Dining at Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel\n\n## Enjoy Food and Drinks Offered at Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel\n\nAt This Hotel\n\n![M9 Restaurant - Buffet](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## M9 Buffet Restaurant\n\nMultiple cuisines\n\nEmbark on a global culinary journey at M9 Buffet Restaurant, the perfect starting point for food lovers. Our buffet offers an exceptional array of international flavors, open daily from breakfast to dinner. Join us and discover your next favorite dish.\n\n![Huang Hao Chinese Restaurant](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## Huang Hao Chinese Restaurant\n\nChinese\n\nWhether combo meals or prix fixe, Huang Hao Chinese Restaurant, downtown Kaohsiung restaurants​ near Love River, offers an authentic cuisine experience. Savor a wide variety of dishes, including the must-try roast duck.\n\n![Kyo Raku Japanese Cuisine & Hot Pot](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## Kyo Raku Japanese Cuisine\n\nJapanese\n\nDiscover the elegance of Japanese at Kyo Raku, led by Chef Masanori Watanabe. Rooted in traditional craftsmanship and seasonal ingredients, we offers exquisite Kaiseki and beautifully presented creations that deliver a memorable culinary experience.\n\n![The Lounge](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## The Lounge\n\nMultiple cuisines\n\nThe Lounge offers a sunlit, sophisticated setting to unwind. Enjoy premium coffee, handcrafted pastries, seasonal afternoon tea, light meals, and signature drinks. Perfect for business, social gatherings, or relaxing amid live piano performances.\n\n![King Chui Hong Kong Café](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## King Chui Hong Kong Cafe\n\nAsian\n\nStep into a vibrant dining destination that celebrates the rich flavors and traditions of Hong Kong. King Chui presents over 40 handcrafted dim sum varieties, chef-curated wok specialties, comforting congee, and classic HK-style milk tea.\n\n![Kyo Kyo Japanese Buffet Restaurant](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## Kyo Kyo Vegetarian Restaurant\n\nVegetarian\n\nWhether you are a vegetarian by choice or simply looking to discover something different, Kyo Kyo, this downtown Kaohsiung vegetarian restaurant, invites you to experience how plant-based cuisine can be vibrant, innovative, and deeply satisfying.\n\n![M-Chef Steak & Teppanyaki](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## M-CHEF TEPPANYAKI\n\nJapanese\n\nM Chef Teppanyaki showcases premium seasonal ingredients, masterful teppan craftsmanship, and live culinary artistry. Enjoy an immersive dining experience where every dish is prepared to perfection.\n\n![Majesty Restaurant](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## Majesty Steak House\n\nSteakhouse\n\nAt Majesty Steak House, we give the classic steakhouse a modern twist. This Kaohsiung city center restaurant uses fresh, top-quality ingredients and bold flavors. Enjoy juicy steaks and creative bistro dishes where tradition meets innovation.\n\n![T-Fusion](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## T-Fusion\n\nCoffee House\n\nBy day, savor specialty coffee and comforting café favorites; by night, unwind with handcrafted cocktails and live jazz. T-FUSION offers an effortlessly elegant day-to-night experience high above the city.\n\n![M Bread & Pastry](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\n## M Bread ＆ Pastry\n\nOther\n\nM Bread & Pastry offers artisan breads, handcrafted cakes, delicate pastries, and seasonal specialties. Made with premium ingredients and expert craftsmanship, every creation brings warmth, flavor, and everyday indulgence.\n\n## Offers & Packages\n\nMORE OPTIONS NEARBY\n\nChinese\n\nDin Tai Fung (鼎泰豐)\n\n2.0 KM\n\n### Frequently Asked Questions\n\nThe on-property restaurants at Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel are:\n\nYes, room service is available at Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel.\n\nThe cuisines offered at Kaohsiung Marriott Hotel are:\n\nYes, breakfast is available onsite at M9 Buffet Restaurant and M Bread ＆ Pastry for a fee.\n\n![Marriott Bonvoy app logo](data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAA4AAAAKCAYAAACE2W/HAAAAFklEQVR42mN8/fpLPQMZgHFU48jWCAD/vyDle0zFdgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==)\n\nUnlock your stay with the Marriott Bonvoy™ App\n\n## KAOHSIUNG MARRIOTT® HOTEL\n\nNo. 222, Long Desin Rd., Gushan District,\n\nKaohsiung, Taiwan, 804\n\nFax: +886 7-558-8566\n\n## Top Destinations\n\n## For Guests\n\n## Our Company\n\n© 1996 – 2026 Marriott International, Inc.       "title": "Places to Eat (Kaohsiung) — Fulbright Taiwan ETA Scholarship",
      "content": "Name: Cheese Me  \nAddress: No. 52 Wenheng 2nd Road, Lingya District / 苓雅區文橫二路52號  \nFavorite dish(es):Hakka Salted Pork w. Sauerkraut Panini; Basil Pesto Chicken & Bacon Panini; Chicken with Teriyaki Sauce Panini; vegetarian dishes available [...] Name: 石頭日式燒肉 (Japanese BBQ)  \nAddress: No. 481號, Dexian Road, Nanzi District, Kaohsiung City, 811  \nFavorite dish(es): Beef Slices  \nReview: This is an all-you-can-eat restaurant, ~$500 NTD for 2 hours. You BBQ the meat and vegetables yourself on a grill in front of you. They also serve sushi. Drinks are also included. They have an English menu. This is a review for the Nanzi branch, but this restaurant is a chain, so there maybe a branch closer to you, just google the restaurant name. [...] Name: Michino’s Diner  \nAddress: 2 locations: 7 Minzu 2nd Road or 79 Datong 1st Road, Lingya District, Kaohsiung  \nFavorite dish(es): Breakfast burrito, omelets, and quesadillas  \nReview: Standard American style restaurant serving American style breakfast and lunch options. Recommend the breakfast set that comes with unlimited tea and coffee refills, fresh juice, and fresh fruit. Closest thing to a real Mexican breakfast burrito near the Cultural Center apartment. English menu.",
      "score": 0.49582586,
      "raw_content": "[Skip to Content](#page) \n\n[![Fulbright Taiwan ETA Scholarship](//images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56381eaae4b00f40082214a6/0c390537-89e2-4a60-9cd4-cb8395e71245/English-White.png?format=1500w)](/)\n\n[![Fulbright Taiwan ETA Scholarship](//images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56381eaae4b00f40082214a6/d1591404-5241-40a0-863c-5fe163a9b88d/English-White.png?format=1500w)](/)\n\n[home](https://taiwan-etaprogram.org/)\n\n[![Fulbright Taiwan ETA Scholarship](//images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56381eaae4b00f40082214a6/0c390537-89e2-4a60-9cd4-cb8395e71245/English-White.png?format=1500w)](/)\n\n[![Fulbright Taiwan ETA Scholarship](//images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56381eaae4b00f40082214a6/d1591404-5241-40a0-863c-5fe163a9b88d/English-White.png?format=1500w)](/)\n\n[home](https://taiwan-etaprogram.org/)\n\n\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56381eaae4b00f40082214a6/1460455363728-JKQP7S2ERTRKTTZMQXIO/UEFPXzM5MTizcb1aMDEuSlBH.JPG)\n\n# Kaohsiung County Eats\n\nEating out in Kaohsiung is inexpensive, delicious, and convenient. Some ETAs enjoy cooking, but beware that powerful ovens like you’re used to are not widely available in Taiwan.\n\nAs a large city, Kaohsiung has many restaurant options and many different cuisines. Besides Chinese and Taiwanese food, Kaohsiung has many Thai, Japanese, and Korean options. \"Western\" food in Taiwan tends to differ from that of the U.S., but likewise, the U.S. certainly doesn't have soup dumplings, oyster omelets, and fried rice like those in Kaohsiung.\n\n**Name:** Lee & Daughters  \n**Address:** 133 Ersheng 2nd Road, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Egg skillets (winter menu), Salmon Benedict French Toast (spring menu)  \n**Review:** Hands down our favorite brunch place in Kaohsiung! Owned by a team of sisters and specializing in the local organic ingredients, everything at this small brunch restaurant is handmade and amazingly delicious. Be prepared to wait, however, as it is very popular and seating is very limited, especially on the weekends. No reservations and no English menu available, but the staff is very helpful.\n\n**Name:** Panchos  \n**Address:** No. 124, Xintian Road, Xinxing District, Kaohsiung City, 800  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Nachos, Taco Salad, Tortilla Chips  \n**Review:** Does Pancho’s have the best Mexican food you ever tasted? No. Does Pancho’s have the most authentic Mexican food you ever tasted? No. Does it have the best, authentic Mexican food at a convenient location in Kaohsiung? Yes. When you miss tortilla chips, when you want the crunchy goodness of an actual tortilla bowl, when you crave black beans served as a vegetable and not a desert, Pancho’s is the place.\n\n**Name:** Gien Jia  \n**Address:** No. 107-1, Jinmen Street, Xinxing District, Kaohsiung City, 800  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Tiramisu, House Salad, Poached Chicken Pasta, Truffle Fries, French Bread  \n**Review:** Gien Jia could hold its own against an artisanal Italian place back in the States. It has a beautiful interior, solid drink menu, and Italian dishes made with fresh produce. The only caveats are that it is comparably expensive with comparably small portions. Very much like an artisanal Italian place back in the States.\n\n**Name:** Ba Fang Yun Ji Dumping  \n**Address:** 高雄市新興區復興一路11號  \n**Review:** Ba Fang Yun Ji Dumpling is a dumpling chain restaurant with many locations. Notably there is one behind the Cultural Center. Its dumplings are cheap, fast, and delicious.\n\n**Name:** Michino’s Diner  \n**Address:** 2 locations: 7 Minzu 2nd Road or 79 Datong 1st Road, Lingya District, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Breakfast burrito, omelets, and quesadillas  \n**Review:** Standard American style restaurant serving American style breakfast and lunch options. Recommend the breakfast set that comes with unlimited tea and coffee refills, fresh juice, and fresh fruit. Closest thing to a real Mexican breakfast burrito near the Cultural Center apartment. English menu.\n\n**Name:** Maharaja Indian Restaurant  \n**Address:** 392 Mincheng 2nd Road, Zuoying District, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Saag Paneer, Baingan Bharta (Eggplant), Lamb Korma  \n**Review:** The best and most authentic Indian food in Kaohsiung. Recommend going with a group though in order to get the most for your money. 3 main dishes usually serves 4 people. Nice atmosphere, English menu, and can accommodate 10+ people.\n\n**Name:** Silver Spoon Thai Hot Pot  \n**Address:** 3F 262 Wufu 2nd Road, Xinsing District, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Green Curry and Lemon Grass Hot Pot  \n**Review:** Great for a group. This restaurant serves Thai style hot pot and offers unlimited drinks and hot pot add-ins for one hour from the time you order. If coming from the MRT, get out at Exit 2 and go up to the 3rd floor of the President Department Store. The restaurant is directly across from a Fitness Factory gym.\n\n**Name:** Cheese Me  \n**Address:** No. 52 Wenheng 2nd Road, Lingya District / 苓雅區文橫二路52號  \n**Favorite dish(es):**Hakka Salted Pork w. Sauerkraut Panini; Basil Pesto Chicken & Bacon Panini; Chicken with Teriyaki Sauce Panini; vegetarian dishes available  \n**Review:** Cheese Me is a great spot for when you’re craving affordable Western style food that consistently tastes good. It’s safe to say this is the only panini-exclusive restaurant in Kaohsiung. The owner/head cook is a Taiwanese American who grew up and lived in California before setting up shop in Kaohsiung. He’s open to feedback on his recipes and can make sandwiches to order, too. When you upgrade any sandwich to a “Super Set” for $59 you also get a drink, soup and dessert. With a superset, the most expensive meal falls just short of $200. Cheese Me is in Lingya District (near 85 tower) and in the middle of the night market. It’s open a bit later than most Taiwanese take-out places, and is more substantial than night market grub. There is limited seating at the bar, but most people order take-out or delivery so there’s usual space.\n\n**Name:** Super Fast Food Delivery /超人外送  \n**Address:** No. 224 Zhongshan 2nd Road, Lingya District /苓雅區中山二路224號  \n**Favorite dish(es):**Grilled Chicken and Bean Burrito; Mexican Style Fries; Grilled Mushroom Cheeseburger  \n**Review:** Super Fast Food Delivery has some of the best cheap Tex Mex food in Kaohsiung. They also have a variety of pizzas, burgers, hotdogs and fries. You will not get this much tasty variety for so little anywhere else in the city. The owner is another California transplant to Kaohsiung who wanted to bring authentic Californian fast food to Taiwan. All the meats for the hot dogs are imported and fries sauce (with the exception of ketchup) is made in house. Also, there’s free water provided. This restaurant opened in late 2014/early 2015, so they haven’t had a chance to renovate the dining area, meaning it’s still pretty bare bones. There’s a small table and some benches outside that could seat up to three people, and a few regular-sized booths in the quiet, back dining room. By no means is this a fancy place to bring a hot date, but it’s definitely a place to bring your homesickness and indulge on the yummy flavors of hard-to-find American cuisine without breaking the bank. Available for delivery to Minquan dwellers.\n\n**Name:** Legends Lunchcart  \n**Address:** 1 Lane 46, XinGuang Road, Lingya District  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Legends Burger; Pastrami Sandwich; Tomato Basil Soup  \n**Review:** Legends Lunchcart (or just “Legends”) is a restaurant across the street from both 85 Skytower and the new public library (in the same building as Mr. O. Gelato). They specialize in sandwiches, hamburgers, grilled cheeses, salads and soups. Legends can easily become one of your favorite daytime or evening expat hangout spots. The owners and staff are boisterous and friendly, and they make great food. Legends can seem a bit pricier than similar Western restaurants (like Super Fast Food Delivery), but the atmosphere is more energetic and homey when it’s a bit crowded. Sometimes, Legends will host special events like “Greek Night” or nighttime boat parties. If you want more information on special events, ask for Sky or Henry.\n\n**Name:** Long Shifu / 龍師傅  \n**Address:** Near 60 Dehang Street, Cishan district (just down the street from the intersection with 7-11, on the opposite side of the street from the shoe store)  \n**Favorite dish(es):**Fried fish with vegetables; Sweet Orange fried chicken with vegetables  \n**Review:** This is a nice buffet-style Taiwanese lunchbox place in Cishan, good for a sit-down meal or take-out.  If you ever need a quick place to eat decent Taiwanese fried chicken (sweet or salty), this is the place to go. They have reliable hours and consistently good food. Vegetarian meals go for under $100 whereas three sides, white rice and a meat, fish or eggs will cost you $100 or more. Free sweet, black tea provided with all meals. The owner, Long Shifu, speaks some English and is usually in the kitchen cooking. If you ever need some help ordering or have any questions, feel free to ask.\n\n**Name:** Woopen  \n**Address:** No. 8-4, Qingnian 1st Road, Lingya District, Kaohsiung City, 802  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Any of their salads, especially the chicken salad  \n**Review:** A delicious place to eat near the Cultural Center, it’s great to get a salad when you want to eat healthy food. In addition to salads, they offer bagels and sandwiches, but I recommend going here for their salads. They have an English translation on their menu, too.\n\n**Name:** 華饌精緻麵食館  \n**Address:** No. 79, Wufu 1st Rd, Lingya District, Kaohsiung City, Taiwan 802  \n**Favorite dish(es):** 小籠湯包 (soup dumplings), 生菜沙拉捲餅 (vegetable and salad roll), 牛肉麵 (beef noodle soup)  \n**Review:** Very close to the Cultural Center, this place is delicious and affordable. Come with friends so you can order multiple dishes to try many things on the menu. There are usually a lot of people/families, but the food comes out pretty quickly. They also offer complementary soymilk and winter melon tea, but depending on your taste buds, it may be too sweet. They also have an English menu in case you need it.\n\n**Name:** [TEN屋](http://www.tenya.com.tw/store.php)  \n**Address:** 813高雄市博愛二路777號 漢神巨蛋4F (We went to the one at Hanshin Department Store up near Kaohsiung Main Arena, but there are multiple locations throughout the country. See website for more details.)  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Any of their okonomiyaki  \n**Review:** If you’re a fan of okonomiyaki (which are grilled savory pancakes with eggs, vegetables and meat inside), look no further! They’re delicious and great to share with friends as you can order multiple okonomiyaki and try a little bit of everything. In addition to okonomiyaki, they also have noodles, rice omelettes, rice bowls and more! A bit on the pricier side, but the taste is worth it. I recommend getting okonomiyaki with noodles inside. The only downside is that there is no English menu, however if you can read Japanese (as there is Japanese on the menu) or simply ask the staff in Chinese, you’ll be fine.\n\n**Name:** 厚得福湯包麵食專賣店  \n**Address:** No. 156, Ziqiang 3rd Road, Lingya District, Kaohsiung City, 802  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Any of their dumplings, 牛肉捲餅, 甜烙餅,  \n**Review:** Located in the Ziqiang Night Market, it’s in between the Sanduo and Central Park MRT stations. Another great family-style Chinese restaurant, you can’t go wrong with any of their dishes! They also have an English menu if you ask for it.\n\n**Name:** 鼠尾草 (Sage)  \n**Address:** 高雄市美濃區中山路2段635號  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Hokkaido (Japanese style) hot pot  \n**Review:** Sage is one of the best hot pot restaurants in Meinong. Their most popular menu items are the hot pot dishes, which you can order in a variety of Southeast Asian flavored broths. The portions are big, and most meals come with rice, vegetables, and a special dipping sauce. In addition, they also offer a few fried rice and vegetarian dishes.\n\n**Name:** K-town Diner  \n**Address:**No. 403號, Anji Street, Zuoying District Kaohsiung City, 813  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Canadian Breakfast  \n**Review:** Bacon! Need I say more? K-town is an American diner that specializes in western style breakfast and brunch. There you can find french toast, bacon, hash browns, omelets, apple pie and a variety of a la carte options—just in case you want to order something that doesn’t come with your meal. They also serve western style burgers, sandwiches and pastas.\n\n**Name:** Meinong traditional Hakka restaurants #1 / Meinong traditional Hakka restaurants #2  \n**Address:** Jhongshan Rd., 362 – Sec 1, Meinong Township, Kaohsiung County / No. 80, Lane 421, Sec. 2, Zhongshan Rd., Meinong Township, Kaohsiung County  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Ban tiao  \n**Review:** If you’re ever in in the mood for a traditional Hakka meal in a bucolic setting, take a trip to Meinong. At these restaurants, you can indulge in authentic Hakka cuisine and traditional Hakka tea, also known as Lei cha. Lei cha is a combination of herbs, nuts, tea leaves, and grains pounded together and mixed with green tea, served as a cold refreshing drink. The main dish in most Hakka meals consist of thick noodles made from rice flour, called ban tiao. You can order ban tiao fried, boiled, cold or hot with a topping of stewed pork lard and/or fried onions. Other popular traditional dishes include stir-fried pig intestines, wild lotus and steamed winter melon.\n\n**Name:** 和楓牛奶堂 (He Feng Milk Hotpot)  \n**Address:** 825高雄縣橋頭鄉白樹村樹德路66號 (close to the Ciaotou MRT stop)  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Pumpkin Milk Hotpot  \n**Review:** This is the most delicious milk hotpot you will find in Kaohsiung. The owners have a farm, so they use fresh milk in their dishes, and some people even just go there to buy gallons of milk. The place is small-ish, maybe can seat 30 people at a time, so if you have a big party, best to reserve ahead of time. Menu is in Chinese.\n\n**Name:** 羅師傅麵飯館  (Master Luo's Restaurant)  \n**Address:** 高雄市鼓山區濱海一路25號 (in Siziwan Bay)  \n**Favorite dish(es):**Stir-fried beef with scallion, fried beef dumplings  \n**Review:** Incredibly delicious, super cheap stir-fried food. They have an English menu, and free tea/soy milk.\n\n**Name:** 石頭日式燒肉 (Japanese BBQ)  \n**Address:** No. 481號, Dexian Road, Nanzi District, Kaohsiung City, 811  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Beef Slices  \n**Review:** This is an all-you-can-eat restaurant, ~$500 NTD for 2 hours. You BBQ the meat and vegetables yourself on a grill in front of you. They also serve sushi. Drinks are also included. They have an English menu. This is a review for the Nanzi branch, but this restaurant is a chain, so there maybe a branch closer to you, just google the restaurant name.\n\n**Name:** 楊寶寶 (Yang Bao Bao Dumplings)  \n**Address:** No. 100, Chaoming Rd, Nanzi District, Kaohsiung City, 811  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Beef soup dumpling, pickled cucumber, chicken soup  \n**Review:** This restaurant is so renowned in Nanzi that people travel to Nanzi just to eat there. It is cheap, has fast service, and is a large restaurant, so even if there is a wait time, it usually isn't more than five minutes. They also have an English menu.\n\n**Name:** EAT  \n**Address:** 400 Zhenghe South Road, Qianzhen District, Kaohsiung 806 / 806高雄市前鎮區鄭和南路400號  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Cheeseburger with caramelized onions, Prosciutto sandwich, chicken Caesar burger  \n**Review:** EAT is a solid East-West Fusion restaurant a minutes-walk away from the Minquan apartment. Fare includes Asian-style rice/veggie/meat combos, along with a variety of Western-style sandwiches, burgers, and salads. The prices are reasonable, the service is good, and the food is high quality. Also, it's owned by one of Fonda's friends, and when she’s working the kitchen prepares for one of the best-tasting, most elegantly crafted burgers you’ve ever had.\n\n**Name:** True Pancake  \n**Address:** 202 Siwei Road, Lingya District, Kaohsiung 802 / 802高雄市苓雅區四維三路202號  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Veggie and soup pancakes, shepherd's pie pancake combo, fried chicken pancake combo, anything served with their pana cotta fruit salad  \n**Review:** True Pancake serves arguably the best pancake breakfast in all of Kaohsiung, with a wide variety of both sweet and savory pancake combos. For fruit lovers, the pana cotta/cream fruit side is also a treat. What’s more, the interior is charmingly decorated, the coffee and tea drinks are fantastic, and the restaurant has a great location just a short walk from the San Duo Shopping District. There’s only one problem—the very \"Taiwanese\" service at the restaurant leaves a lot to be desired. If the restaurant is busy (as it often is on weekends), they'll just give you a standard \"no seats for an hour\" response, even if one table is just about to be finished eating. They also hold tables for reservations for far too long, which is especially aggravating when you’re starving on a late Sunday morning. All this can make going to True Pancake a bit of a 麻煩 (bothersome) experience, but if you're aching for a (pan)caking, it's well worth the effort.\n\n**Name:** Elegant Herb Garden  \n**Address:** Lane 134 Zhongshan Road, Dashu District, Kaohsiung / 大樹區水寮里中山路134巷  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Basil chicken pesto pasta, pumpkin vegetarian pasta, BBQ pork ribs, tuna crackers, pretty much any of their teas.  \n**Review:** The Elegant Herb Garden an absolute gem tucked away in the farm-filled Dashu district, about 30 minutes from central Kaohsiung. Make the commute and you'll be rewarded with mouth-watering Western-style dishes, delicious teas, and one of the prettiest (and greenest) restaurant atmospheres I've ever seen. It's also locally famous for organically growing its own herbs and spices, giving the food a freshness that's difficult to find elsewhere. A truly wonderful way to escape the hustle and bustle of the city on a sunny afternoon.\n\n**Name:** Andy’s Pizza Garden  \n**Address:** No. 273 Yucheng Street, Zuoying District, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Margherita Pizza, Andy’s Supreme Calzone  \n**Review:** This is one of the only legitimate pizza restaurants in Kaohsiung. One of the owners is a foreigner and his son is his young apprentice and together they cook delicious pizzas and calzones right out of a real brick oven! Strongly recommended.\n\n**Name:** General Sushi  \n**Address:** No. 38 Mingfeng third Street, Qianzhen District, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Pork/chicken rice, shrimp roll, crab roll  \n**Review:** It’s kind out of town but this is some of the best sushi in Kaohsiung. This restaurant is well worth the trip, especially for an ETA possibly working out in Siaogang. It’s extremely popular though, so try to call ahead and make a reservation or get there right at opening time.\n\n**Name:** 檸檬草香茅火鍋專賣店 Lemongrass Hot Pot  \n**Address:** No. 250號, Xintian Road, Qianjin District, Kaohsiung City  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Curry hot pot, tomato hot pot, thai hot and sour hot pot  \n**Review:** Allegedly the original Kaohsiung lemongrass hot pot spot, this charming restaurant has wonderfully kind and helpful staff as well as amazing hot pot. The menu is a little confusingly organized so just know that you have to order: 1) broth 2) meat 3) carb (for example, rice, noodles, etc) and 4) sauce. For sauce, we highly recommend the third combo option on the menu- it totally makes the meal! This is an awesome affordable hot pot restaurant right around the central park area.\n\n**Name:** Smokey Joe’s  \n**Address:** 800高雄市新興區忠孝一路208號 (just one of three locations)  \n**Favorite dish(es):** For a flat burger go here  \n**Review:** This restaurant is included exclusively as a warning to future ETAs. Many people, knowing you are a foreigner, will recommend this destination to you for its alleged western cuisine. Enter into one of its two over the top, smothered in cliché décor venues and you will discover a pathetic attempt at food you vaguely recognize from home. Recommended only for the truly desperate (however, with Kaohsiung’s many restaurant options, it’s hard to ever get that desperate).\n\n**Name:** Mr. O Gelato  \n**Address:** 802高雄市苓雅區新光路48-62號 802高雄市苓雅區新光路48-62號  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Fudge Chocolate  \n**Review:** This is probably the best gelato in Kaohsiung. The small, heartbreaking caveat is that Mr. O closes painfully early, usually too early for post-dinner dessert. However, the flavors are all amazing and generous helping served.\n\n**Name:** “Chinese Burrito Stand”  \n**Address:** Southeast corner of Wenheng Second Road and Wenheng Second Road Lane Nine  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Chinese chicken burrito, original or Thai style- add cheese and egg!  \n**Review:** There can be no higher review than two Americans eating Chinese burritos from this stand twice a week every week for six months. Not much else to say here but that every person who tastes the golden burrito, the Chinese burrito, falls in love with it. An adorable married couple runs this stand and will likely remember your “regular” within two trips.\n\n**Name:** Jacaranda  \n**Address:**No. 72-5, Wenzhong Rd, Gushan District, Kaohsiung City, Taiwan 807  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Margarita pizza  \n**Review:** Jacaranda is a great pizza joint located in Northern Kaohsiung by Houyi station. Sit down and enjoy a delicious deep-dish pizza (even the Chicagoans rave about it) while sipping on a cool craft beer. Not in the mood? The menu offers a wide variety of salads and other comfort foods. Note: you cannot get pizza until after 6:00PM. Closed on Thursdays.\n\n**Name:** Linjia Dumpling Stand  \n**Address:** No. 386 Guanghua Second Road, Lingya District, Kaohsiung  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Xiao Long Bao  \n**Review:** Want an easy meal to go? Why not try Linjia dumplings? Delicious and fast, these handmade dumplings are made right before your eyes from scratch. Great snack after school or for a quick dinner. I also suggest the xianbing if you’re in the mood for something tasty! Vegetables are also served there and the owner is actually the president of the Guanghua night market.\n\n**Name:** Din Tai Feng  \n**Address:** B1, No. 777, Bo-ai 2nd Rd., Zuoying Dist., Kaohsiung City(Hanshin Arena Shopping Plaza)  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Fried rice  \n**Review:** All the foreigners love this restaurant. No reservations and you may have to wait for up to three hours in Taipei (about 45 in Kaohsiung), but believe me it’s worth the wait. Xiaolongbao, fried rice, sauteed spinach, and the string bean dish are all a must. If you’re looking to impress your friends from home with a nice meal, this is the place to go.\n\n**Name:** Neighborhood Chinese Restaurant  \n**Address:** corner of Yide and Fuxing  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Mapo Tofu  \n**Review:** Want to share a good meal with friends? Look no further than the corner of Yide and Fuxing where you can get a wide variety of seafood dishes or my personal favorite, Mapo Tofu. Also a fun place to go on Monday nights during the night market where the restaurant and street will be hopping.\n\n**Name:** Vicky Foods – Burritos and Indian Food  \n**Address:** Houchang Road, Nanzi District, Kaohsiung City, Kaohsiung, Taiwan 811  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Beef burrito  \n**Review:** This place is worth going to Nanzi for. It is a hole in the wall and easy to miss. It's owned by an Indian guy and his family and is open for lunch and dinner. Be sure to get there early though, because he closes at 7:30. The Beef Burrito is made with good Australian beef. It is definitely a fusion of Mexican and Indian flavors—a unique dining experience. Beloved by Nanzi ETAs. Also only 70 NT.\n\n**Name:** Chun Shui Tang 春水堂  \n**Address:** No. 322, Hedi Road, Sanmin District, Kaohsiung City, Taiwan 807  \n**Favorite dish(es):** any tea option  \n**Review:** This joint is a Taiwanese tea house. The food is really tasty, but the tea is even better Actually to die for. So tasty—you can't go wrong. The food is Taiwanese cuisine and a la carte, perfect for a snack/study place or a heavy meal. Think upscale Taiwanese food. The price is a bit more expensive than most Taiwanese places, but cheaper than most foreign food.\n\n**Name:** 小王牛肉麵  \n**Address:** 高雄市新興區六合一路94-1號  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Beef Noodles  \n**Review:** The staff is very kind, and the beef noodles are famous. They have won many awards for being the best in Taiwan.\n\n**Name:** 城市部落  \n**Address:** 高雄市左營區自由三路551-1號  \n**Favorite dish(es):** Try all the different sorts of indigenous food—the roasted pig is especially good  \n**Review:** This place serves a fusion of Chinese and indigenous food. You have to call ahead to make a reservation, and typically they will ask you how large your party is so they know how much pig to roast. You can order all sorts of dishes to be shared family style. As you eat, there are performances of indigenous dancing, in which they call for customers to join in.\n\n[Back to Kaohsiung Overview](http://taiwan-etaprogram.org/kh-overview/)",

Places to Eat in Kaohsiung, Taiwan 🇹🇼 Details 👇🏻  \n  \n📍 SAVE THIS Post for Your Next Trip!  \n  \nCAN YOU believe the prices!?   \n  \nTraveling to Kaohsiung, Taiwan? This city is a hidden gem for foodies, with incredible local eats and restaurants. These Kaohsiung food spots come highly visited by locals and some tourists —don’t miss them!  \n  \n1️⃣ Xing Jong Lu  \nStart your day with their famous shaobing (sesame flatbread stuffed with fried bread, eggs, and greens) and juicy tangbao (soup dumplings). Trust me, these are a must-try! 🥟  \n  \n2️⃣ Chunlan Gua Bao (春蘭割包)  \nThese oversized steamed buns are packed with flavor! The must-try is the assorted gua bao with soy-braised pork, pickled cabbage, peanut sugar, and their secret chili sauce. Even a plant-based version for vegans! 🌱  \n  \n3️⃣ Caizong Li  \nOnly two items on the menu! Vegetarian friendly. Their peanut zongzi (sticky rice dumplings) and kelp miso soup are simple yet unforgettable. Perfect for a light, flavorful meal. 🍙  \n  \n4️⃣ Cianjin Braised Pork  \nA local favorite! Their braised pork rice is legendary, and the milkfish soup is a must-try. You’ll see why this spot is always packed. 🐟  \n  \n5️⃣ Angel’s Fried Chicken @ Ruifeng Night Market  \nEnd your food adventure at Taiwan’s iconic night market. Angel’s Fried Chicken is a standout, but honestly, you can’t go wrong with any vendor here. 🍗  \n  \n💬 Which spot are you adding to your Kaohsiung food bucket list? There is usually a wait at this place, but the noodles are worth it.\n Duck Zhen (鴨肉珍) - We got the minced braised duck rice, organ soup, and a side of vegetables. There are a lot of other duck dishes to choose from. A famous and easy spot for a quick meal.\n 張媽媽台灣魯肉飯 - Quiet place for some Taiwanese staples near the National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts. We got an Earl Grey and 肉鬆 (pork floss) donut.\n Caffe Artigiano (艾奇諾咖啡) - Solid iced coffees for a hot day (and sighting of a neighborhood cat).\n Delicatesse - Got the chocolate croissant at the bakery next to the hotel we stayed at. Shoutout to Kathy Cheng (Tricky Taipei) for this recommendation!\n\nThings to Do:\n\nDragon and Tiger Pagodas\n\nDragon and Tiger Pagodas\n\nMuseum of Fine Arts\n\nMuseum of Fine Arts\n\nWeiwu Mimi Village (衛武迷迷村)\n\nWeiwu Mimi Village (衛武迷迷村)\n\nWhite Gourd & Fat Guy (冬瓜與胖子)\n\nWhite Gourd & Fat Guy (冬瓜與胖子)",

      "raw_content": "[Skip to Content](#page) \n\n[FELICIA LIANG](/)\n\n[FELICIA LIANG](/)\n\n\n\n# What To Do In Kaohsiung, Taiwan\n\n[Travel](/blog/category/Travel)[Taiwan](/blog/category/Taiwan)\n\nWritten By [Felicia Liang](/blog?author=58de700aebbd1a43e99388e8)\n\nKaohsiung is Taiwan’s third largest city and the country’s largest port city. I went for the first time in 2009, but it has changed a lot since then, particularly with its many large public art spaces perfect for exploring. It was wonderful to visit and definitely an underrated place with hidden gems. Kaohsiung also has an MRT and a light rail, so while the city is more spread out, it was pretty convenient to get around.\n\nEverything captured here were found via Google Maps and recommendations from friends. Below are some food highlights, top things I enjoyed doing, plus places I have marked for next time.\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5e5ed1a1054c294ef27d2898/d0daee82-148f-4659-81c9-65f959d83a86/Taiwan_Journal_Kaohsiung.png)\n\n**Places to eat:**\n\n* **Gang Yuan Beef Noodle Restaurant (港園牛肉麵)** - Got their dry noodles that still had a rich beef broth flavor (with the actual broth on the side), and then washed everything down with watermelon juice. There is usually a wait at this place, but the noodles are worth it.\n* **Duck Zhen (鴨肉珍**) - We got the minced braised duck rice, organ soup, and a side of vegetables. There are a lot of other duck dishes to choose from. A famous and easy spot for a quick meal.\n* **張媽媽台灣魯肉飯** - Quiet place for some Taiwanese staples near the National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts. The 麵線 (thin noodles) were a standout.\n* **White Gourd & Fat Guy (冬瓜與胖子)** - A couple, who call each other White Gourd and Fat Guy, bought an actual home in Kaohsiung and converted it into a restaurant with 2 seating times. We were served a traditional, home-style Taiwanese dinner with whatever ingredients they had in the kitchen. We had a five course meal plus a soup for only 350 NTD. It’s a cozy and funkily decorated place and a definite recommendation if you’re going to Kaohsiung!\n\n**Places to snack and have coffee:**\n\n* **Dough DoughNut** **(兜兜圈)** - Fun donut shop tucked in a tiny alley that usually sells out before day's end! We got an Earl Grey and 肉鬆 (pork floss) donut.\n* **Caffe Artigiano** **(艾奇諾咖啡)** - Solid iced coffees for a hot day (and sighting of a neighborhood cat).\n* **Delicatesse** - Got the chocolate croissant at the bakery next to the hotel we stayed at. Shoutout to Kathy Cheng ([Tricky Taipei](https://www.trickytaipei.com)[)](https://www.trickytaipei.com) for this recommendation!\n\n**Things to Do:**\n\n![Dragon and Tiger Pagodas]()\n\nDragon and Tiger Pagodas\n\n![Museum of Fine Arts]()\n\nMuseum of Fine Arts\n\n![Weiwu Mimi Village (衛武迷迷村)]()\n\nWeiwu Mimi Village (衛武迷迷村)\n\n![White Gourd & Fat Guy (冬瓜與胖子)]()\n\nWhite Gourd & Fat Guy (冬瓜與胖子)\n\n* **Kaohsiung Museum of Fine Arts -** The standout at this place was seeing oil paintings with Asian faces and people, as I’m so accustomed to seeing European faces in that style, and seeing southern Taiwan pride show through in the culture and art (one of its permanent exhibitions is called South Plus).\n* **Pier 2 Art Center** -Abandoned warehouse site that’s been turned to an art center with exhibition spaces and shops. Lots of large art installations and murals to see while just talking around the area.\n* **National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts - Weiwuying** - We just walked around here during the day to check out the new concert hall, and caught someone playing the grand piano outdoors in front of the opera house. The Weiwuying Metropolitan Park is nearby that’s nice for walks and greenery too.\n* **Weiwu Mimi Village (衛武迷迷村)** -Neighborhood near Weiwuying with lots of large murals and artworks covering the buildings. As of 2022, there are more than 50 international artists from 24 countries, with 135 painted mural walls.\n* **Dragon and Tiger Pagodas** - One of the many temple at **Lotus Pond**, an artificial lake near the Kaohsiung HSR stop. There are 2 towers, one with a dragon entrance, and the other with a tiger entrance. You enter through the dragon, and exit through the tiger in the hopes of changing your luck. This wasn’t the largest temple by any means, but I think the meaning behind its entrance and exit an interesting one.\n\n**For Next Time:**\n\n* Rueifong Night Market (瑞豐夜市)\n* Cijin Island\n* Marsalis Bar 馬沙里斯爵士酒館\n\n***Tip:*** *Taiwan businesses use Google reviews and Facebook to keep up to date information, so for dinner and drinks at the more popular places, it’s best to call or message them a few days beforehand to make a reservation. Many popular coffee spots also have waits and are generally first come, first serve.*\n\n[Taiwan Travel](/blog/tag/Taiwan+Travel)[Taiwan Eats](/blog/tag/Taiwan+Eats)[Kaohsiung Travel](/blog/tag/Kaohsiung+Travel)[What to do in Kaohsiung](/blog/tag/What+to+do+in+Kaohsiung)[Taiwan Itinerary](/blog/tag/Taiwan+Itinerary)[Travel Illustration](/blog/tag/Travel+Illustration)[Travel Sketchbook](/blog/tag/Travel+Sketchbook)[Food Illustration](/blog/tag/Food+Illustration)[Felicia Liang](/blog/tag/Felicia+Liang)[AAPI Artist](/blog/tag/AAPI+Artist)[Asian American Artist](/blog/tag/Asian+American+Artist)\n\n[![]()  Felicia Liang](/blog?author=58de700aebbd1a43e99388e8)\n\n[Previous\n\n## What To Do In Tainan, Taiwan](/blog/things-to-do-in-tainan-taiwan)  [Next\n\n## What To Do In Taichung, Taiwan](/blog/things-to-do-in-taichung-taiwan)\n\n ",

      "url": "https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/faculty/life-in-kaohsiung",
      "title": "Life in Kaohsiung - I-Shou International School Kaohsiung",
      "content": "## Language\n\nThe official language is Mandarin Chinese, but in southern Taiwan, Taiwanese and Hakka are spoken, as are various aboriginal languages. Even though English is considered the second official language, less than 20% of the Kaohsiung population is able to communicate in English. Recently, there has been increased emphasis on learning English in the public schools, and the results are evident. With a combination of English and Google Translate, you can get by day to day without knowing any Chinese in Kaohsiung.\n\n### Food [...] ### Food\n\nFood assumes such an elevated status in Kaohsiung that people often greet each other with a friendly, “chir bao le mei”, which literally means, “Have you eaten yet?” While not an invitation to dine, the inquiry confirms food as the source for overall well being. Guided by climate and lifestyle, the Kaohsiung palate is both practical and conservative. Cafeteria or buffet style service is preferred, and simple street side settings are most popular. However, there are several establishments that serve international fare and full-course dinners.",
      "score": 0.34786987,
      "raw_content": "[### I-Shou International School Kaohsiung](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/ \"I-Shou International School Kaohsiung\")\n\n* [News](/latest-news)\n  + [Calendar](/latest-news/calendar)\n  + [In Focus](/latest-news/in-focus-news)\n  + [Inquirer](/latest-news/inquirer-news)\n  + [Clips](/latest-news/clips-news)\n* [Students](/students)\n* [Parents](/parents)\n  + [Resources](/parents/resources)\n  + [Transportation](/parents/transportation)\n  + [Uniform](/parents/uniform)\n  + [PTA](/parents/pta)\n* [Faculty & Staff](/faculty)\n  + [Resources](/faculty/resources)\n  + [Faculty Profile](/faculty/faculty-profile)\n  + [Life in Kaohsiung](/faculty/life-in-kaohsiung)\n  + [Employment](/faculty/employment)\n* [Contact](/contact)\n\n* [IB World School](/)\n* [About](/about)\n  + - [Welcome to I-Shou](/about/welcome)\n    - [Vision & Mission](/about/vision)\n    - [History](/about/history)\n    - [Governance & Administration](/about/governance)\n\n    - [Facilities](/about/facilities)\n    - [Accreditation](/about/accreditation)\n    - [Partnerships & Memberships](/about/partnerships)\n    - [Child Protection & Safeguarding](/about/child-protection-and-safeguarding-3)\n* [Admissions](/admissions)\n  + - [Why I-Shou?](/admissions/why-iis)\n    - [Admissions Overview](/admissions/admissions-overview)\n    - [Apply Now](/admissions/apply-now)\n    - [Visit I-Shou](/admissions/visit-iis)\n\n    - [Entry Requirements](/admissions/entry-requirements)\n    - [Fees](/admissions/fees)\n    - [Admissions FAQs](/admissions/faqs)\n    - [School E-brochure](/admissions/e-brochure-en)\n* [Teaching & Learning](/teaching)\n  + - [The IIS Learner](/teaching/iis-learner)\n    - [The IB Programme](/teaching/ib-programme)\n    - [National Curriculum](/teaching/national-curriculum)\n    - [Technology & Innovation](/teaching/technology-innovation)\n\n    - [E.X.P.L.O.R.E. Program](/teaching/global-exploration-trips)\n    - [International Summer Camp](/teaching/international-summer-camp)\n    - [Sports & Activities](/teaching/sports-activities)\n    - [Service Learning](/teaching/service-learning)\n* [School Life](/school-life)\n  + - [School Schedule](/school-life/school-schedule)\n    - [Student Leadership](/school-life/student-leadership)\n    - [Student Support](/school-life/student-support)\n\n    - [Other Services](/school-life/other-services)\n    - [Boarding Life](/school-life/boarding-life)\n* [Academics](/academics)\n  + - [Preschool NK-K2](/academics/preschool)\n    - [Primary Years G1-G5](/academics/g1-g5)\n    - [Middle Years G6-G8](/academics/g6-g8)\n    - [IGCSE G9-G10](/academics/igcse-g9-g10)\n    - [Diploma G11-G12](/academics/g11-g12)\n    - [Secondary Bilingual Programme G7-G12](/academics/g7-g12)\n\n    - [Communication & Reporting](/academics/communication-reporting)\n    - [Pathways](/academics/pathways)\n    - [University admissions](/academics/university-admissions)\n* [Apply Now](/apply-now)\n\nLife in Kaohsiung\n\n# Life in Kaohsiung\n\n## Located in southwestern Taiwan, Kaohsiung is cast in sunshine throughout a year and its weather is amiable. Kaohsiung used to enjoy a thriving economy for its steel and petrochemical industries, but now it is gearing towards cultural tourism, touting its history, nature and hospitality. An oceanic tourist capital it is!\n\nThe southern city of Kaohsiung (高雄; Gāoxióng) is Taiwan's largest port, its second-largest city and center of the country's heavy and petrochemical industries.\n\nToday's Kaohsiung has largely been transformed from grim industrial warrens into a modern urban landscape of airy cafes, wide streets, waterside parks, public transport, bicycle lanes, and cultural venues that have embraced the manufacturing past. There are also two swimming beaches within the city area, and 1000 hectares of almost-pristine forest right on its doorstep.\n\n### Culture\n\nWhen in Taiwan, you will most certainly be amazed at the diversity of things this beautiful island has to offer, as a rich historical background has provided Taiwan with a multifaceted culture. People from many different places and backgrounds, such as Taiwan's indigenous people, the southern Fujianese from early China, Hakka immigrants, the Dutch, Spanish, Japanese, and the recent immigrants from mainland China, have all played a role in Taiwan's development. While gradually developing a new culture indigenous to Taiwan, they also hold on to their respective customs and traditions; as a result, you will be able to sample indigenous, Taiwanese, and Chinese cultures and even find traces left by the Dutch and the Japanese when traveling in Taiwan.\n\n![](/images/site/kaohsiung-taiwan-kaohsiung-confucius-temple-01.jpg)\n\n![](/images/site/2632229915-25ca7b5674-b.jpg)\n\n### Climate\n\nLocated over a degree to the south of the Tropic of Cancer, the climate of Kaohsiung is tropical, specifically a tropical savanna climate (Köppen Aw), with monthly mean temperatures in the range of between 20 to 29 °C (68 to 84 °F) .The humid summer season lasts from May to October. November to February can be cooler, particularly at night. Typically, apartments and commercial buildings do not have heat, which leads to the days feeling chillier than you might expect, especially if you are on a scooter! Often people use space heaters for colder nights. From March or April it begins to get warmer, but it is not as humid as in the summer. Typhoons often occur from June to October. The east coast and northern section of Taiwan receive most of the effects of the typhoons with a storm occasionally passing through the southern portion.\n\n## Health\n\nTaiwan has national health insurance for all residents and international employees. The metropolitan area of Kaohsiung contains over 90 hospitals and 1,178 medical clinics, providing a total of 9,186 beds. There are many health care providers who speak English. Medical and dental care is excellent and unbelievably inexpensive! Most prescriptions can be obtained through the national health insurance.\n\n### Economy\n\nTaiwan’s economy is one of the fastest growing economies in the world and one of the strongest in Asia. Currently, the gross national product (GNP) per capita is over 3 times China’s annual GNP per capita. The currency used in Taiwan is New Taiwan dollars (NT$) and the exchange rate has been very stable at approximately 30 NT$ to 1 US$. Personal checks are not used in Taiwan; credit cards or cash, which can be accessed through numerous ATM machines, without daily limited amounts, are frequently used in Taiwan for all purchases\n\n![](/images/site/the-urban-landscape-1698285-960-720.jpg)\n\n![](/images/site/800px-yujhu-shopping-area-in-kaohsiung-taiwan.jpg)\n\n### Shopping\n\nKaohsiung City has numerous shopping opportunities: day and night markets, where one can find great bargains; beautiful, modern 10-15 story department stores (more than 10 major ones); and countless boutiques. Dream Mall, one of the largest shopping malls in Taiwan, opened in March 2008. In 2010, E-Da World, a shopping and entertainment complex incorporating luxury hotels, an amusement park, and Asia's largest outlet mall, was opened across the street from the I-Shou International School campus. Most well known brands of clothing and shoes can be found here, as well as in all department stores throughout the city. Ikea and Costco are popular places to buy household items, although local, family-owned stores are plentiful and often offer good deals.\n\n## Language\n\nThe official language is Mandarin Chinese, but in southern Taiwan, Taiwanese and Hakka are spoken, as are various aboriginal languages. Even though English is considered the second official language, less than 20% of the Kaohsiung population is able to communicate in English. Recently, there has been increased emphasis on learning English in the public schools, and the results are evident. With a combination of English and Google Translate, you can get by day to day without knowing any Chinese in Kaohsiung.\n\n### Food\n\nFood assumes such an elevated status in Kaohsiung that people often greet each other with a friendly, “chir bao le mei”, which literally means, “Have you eaten yet?” While not an invitation to dine, the inquiry confirms food as the source for overall well being. Guided by climate and lifestyle, the Kaohsiung palate is both practical and conservative. Cafeteria or buffet style service is preferred, and simple street side settings are most popular. However, there are several establishments that serve international fare and full-course dinners.\n\nMeals served in a Chinese fashion are usually one dish at a time, in which all would share. Some of the international cuisines available in Kaohsiung are Italian, French, German, Tex-Mex, Japanese, Thai, and many others. Typical Chinese cuisine is quite different in Taiwan.\n\nBesides excellent restaurants, there are numerous familiar Western eating establishments and coffeehouses: McDonald's, KFC, Subway, Starbucks, and many wonderful bakeries.\n\n![](/images/site/800px-liouho-night-market-27-dec-06.jpg)\n\n![](/images/site/kaohsiung-1742610-960-720.jpg)\n\n### housing\n\nMultiple floor housing complexes dominate the community. Within each complex there is a common area for limited recreation and/or entertainment. Most apartments are well designed given the size, with a good amount of cupboards, drawers and built in furniture. Furnished apartments include basic living, dining and bedroom furniture and kitchen appliances such as a refrigerator, stove top and dish dryer. A microwave and toaster oven will most likely not be included. Kitchens do not typically include dishwashers or ovens. I-Shou IS teachers are offered assistance in locating suitable accommodations, and a housing allowance is provided to those hired overseas. The housing allowance, on average, covers between 70-100% of monthly rental expenses.\n\n## transportation\n\nMany people own cars, but bicycles, buses, motorcycles, scooters and taxis are the principal forms of transport. Trains and buses are typically used for traveling longer distances. Taiwan’s public transportation is among the best in Asia. Translation cards will be helpful to give to taxi drivers. A valid driver’s license is required for operating a vehicle in Taiwan. Check with your home country Representative Office for current regulations and testing procedures.\n\nInternational driver’s licenses are accepted.\n\nInsurance coverage is only provided to those holding a valid license or official endorsement. Driver’s license testing is provided in Kaohsiung; and a driver’s test manual is available in English.\n\nTaxi Service: You can hail a taxi on the street or have your apartment guard call one for you. Charges are NT $85 for the first 1.5 km. and NT $5 for each additional 250 meters. Taxis leaving Kaohsiung airport add NT$50 to the metered fare and charge extra for oversize luggage.\n\n### safety\n\nKaohsiung, and Taiwan in general, is very safe! There are virtually no “bad areas” of town, and violent crime is nearly non-existent. That being said, with a population of more than two million, it is best to exercise the same caution you would in any large city.\n\n![](/images/site/kaohsiung-taiwan-formosa-boulevard-station-2714883.jpg)\n\n## Sightseeing and Attractions\n\nThere are many things to do in Kaohsiung City and nearby areas. For those looking for cultural activities, there is the Kaohsiung Museum of Fine Arts with outdoor sculpture grounds; the Cultural Center, which provides musical tours, plays, and symphonies; the Kaohsiung History Museum, containing a small collection of artifacts and paintings; and many beautiful and historic Buddhist, Confucius, and Taoist temples – some combining elements of all three.\n\nThere is also a Science and Technology Museum and the Kaohsiung Zoo. The Zoo contains more than 100 species of rare and wonderful animals from all over the world. In addition, shaded pathways lead to a number of quaint temples and shrines hidden amidst the forests.\n\nKaohsiung Lantern Festival: At the end of Chinese New Year, near the Kaohsiung harbor and along the Love River, the Kaohsiung Lantern Festival is filled with a variety traditional lanterns and more modern light sculptures, nightly fireworks shows, Taiwanese food stands and other art programs and live concerts.\n\nUrban Spotlight: Located at the corner of Central Park, Urban Spotlight is a cafe with a stage where local live bands perform in the evening. Take a walk around Central Park then stop at the cafe to cool off.\n\nNight Markets: Night markets play a significant role in Taiwanese culture, and Kaohsiung is home to some of the biggest and most vibrant markets in the world. Known for their unique food, clothing and game stalls, these markets, which are scattered throughout the city, are truly an experience you can’t afford to miss.\n\nLotus Lake/Lotus Pond (尾北里): Take a walk or bike around Lotus Lake. The lake is surrounded by temples, some of which have built out onto the lake. Of note are the Dragon-Tiger Pavilion and Spring Autumn Pavilion, as well as Taiwan's largest Confucius temple. During the day the lake is surrounded with stalls selling food, drinks and trinkets. Across the street from the Dragon-Tiger Pavilion is a Taiwanese puppet shop that's worth a look. Just south of the lake is one of the restored gates of the Old Wall of Fongshan, built in 1826. A few blocks down on Shengli Road is the North Gate, the best-preserved of the three. On the other side of the gate is a new park and the Military Dependents' Museum, which features many vintage Taiwanese household items. Another attraction at lotus pond is the wakeboarding park \"Lotus Wakepark\". Take at least half a day to really enjoy this activity, relax after some rounds of wakeboarding in their restaurant Wakey Wakey with some international food or enjoy a cocktail and live music in the evening.\n\nMore links\n\n<https://www.lonelyplanet.com/taiwan/southern-taiwan/kaohsiung-city>\n\n<https://khh.travel/index.aspx?l=2>\n\n![](/images/site/230370651-624-v1520935251.jpg)\n\n![](/images/site/heart-of-love-river-in-kaohsiung-night-view-1885958.jpg)\n\n![](/images/site/flames-light-fire-sparks-heat-rotation-action-265850.jpg)\n\n##### In this Section:\n\n* [Resources](/faculty/resources)\n* [Faculty Profile](/faculty/faculty-profile)\n* [Life in Kaohsiung](/faculty/life-in-kaohsiung)\n* [Employment](/faculty/employment)\n\n![moe](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n![cis](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n![ib](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n![Cambridge International Education](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n![acamis](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n![tissa](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n![Keeping Safe](https://www.iis.kh.edu.tw/images/assets/transparent.gif)\n\n ",

The downtown areas are centered on Kaohsiung Harbor with Cijin Island on the other side of the harbor acting as a natural breakwater \"Breakwater (structure)\"). The Love River (Ai River) flows into the harbor through the Old City and downtown. Zuoying Military Harbor lies to the north of Kaohsiung Harbor and the city center. Kaohsiung's natural landmarks include Ape Hill \"Shoushan (Kaohsiung)\") and Mount Banping.\n\n### Climate\n\n| Kaohsiung |",
