Hepburn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepburn_romanization \"Hepburn romanization\") | Takao-shi |\n| [Kunrei-shiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kunrei-shiki_romanization \"Kunrei-shiki romanization\") | Takao-si |\n\n![Kaohsiung is located in South China Sea](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/South_China_Sea_location_map.svg/330px-South_China_Sea_location_map.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Pratas Island](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Red_pog.svg/20px-Red_pog.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Taiping Island](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Blue_pog.svg/20px-Blue_pog.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Kaohsiung](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Green_pog.svg/20px-Green_pog.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0c/Red_pog.svg/20px-Red_pog.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Blue_pog.svg/20px-Blue_pog.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Green_pog.svg/20px-Green_pog.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n**Kaohsiung**,[[a]](#cite_note-4) officially **Kaohsiung City**,[[II]](#cite_note-5) is a [special municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_municipality_(Taiwan) \"Special municipality (Taiwan)\") located in southern [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan \"Taiwan\"). With a population of approximately 2.7 million people, it is Taiwan's third most populous city and the largest city in [southern Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Taiwan \"Southern Taiwan\").[[3]](#cite_note-sr-6) After annexing the entirety of [Kaohsiung County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_County \"Kaohsiung County\") in 2010, the city's administrative boundaries range from the coastal urban center across wide rural stretches and into the wilderness of the [Yushan Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yushan_Range \"Yushan Range\"), making it the largest city in Taiwan by area at 2,952 km2 (1,140 mi2).[[4]](#cite_note-7)\n\nFounded in the [17th century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/17th_century \"17th century\") as a small trading village named **Tancoia**, a name of uncertain origins,[[b]](#cite_note-8) the city has since grown into the economic center of [southern Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Taiwan \"Southern Taiwan\"), with key industries such as manufacturing, [steel-making](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel-making \"Steel-making\"), [oil refining](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil_refining \"Oil refining\"), [freight transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freight_transport \"Freight transport\") and [shipbuilding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipbuilding \"Shipbuilding\"). It is classified as a \"Sufficiency\" level [global city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city \"Global city\") by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network \"Globalization and World Cities Research Network\"),[[5]](#cite_note-9) with some of the most prominent infrastructures in Taiwan.\n\nKaohsiung is of strategic importance to the nation as the main port city of Taiwan; the [Port of Kaohsiung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Kaohsiung \"Port of Kaohsiung\") is the largest and busiest harbor in Taiwan: more than 67% of the nation's export and import container volume passes through Kaohsiung.[[6]](#cite_note-10) [Kaohsiung International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_International_Airport \"Kaohsiung International Airport\") is the second busiest airport by number of passengers in Taiwan. The city is well-connected to other major cities by [high speed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_High_Speed_Rail \"Taiwan High Speed Rail\") and [conventional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Railway \"Taiwan Railway\") rail, as well as several [national freeways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highway_system_in_Taiwan \"Highway system in Taiwan\"). It also hosts the [Republic of China Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Navy \"Republic of China Navy\") (ROCN) fleet headquarters and its [naval academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Naval_Academy \"Republic of China Naval Academy\"). More recent public works such as [Pier-2 Art Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier-2_Art_Center \"Pier-2 Art Center\"), [National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Kaohsiung_Center_for_the_Arts \"National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts\") and [Kaohsiung Music Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Music_Center \"Kaohsiung Music Center\") have been aimed at growing the tourism and [cultural industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_industries \"Cultural industries\") of the city.\n\n## Etymology\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/Map_of_Taiwan_%28Formosa%29_in_1880%2C_from-_Stanford%27s_map_of_the_empires_of_China_and_Japan_with_the_adjacent_parts_of_the_Russian_Empire%2C_India%2C_Burma_etc._LOC_2006458442_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n[Hoklo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoklo_people \"Hoklo people\") immigrants to the area during the 16th and 17th centuries called the region *Takau* (Chinese: 打狗; Pe̍h-ōe-jī: *Táⁿ-káu*). The surface meaning of the associated [Chinese characters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_characters \"Chinese characters\") was \"beat the dog\". According to one theory, the name \"Takau\" originates from the indigenous [Austronesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_languages \"Austronesian languages\") [Siraya language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siraya_language \"Siraya language\") and translates as \"[bamboo forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamboo_forest \"Bamboo forest\")\". According to another theory, the name evolved via [metathesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metathesis_(linguistics) \"Metathesis (linguistics)\") from the name of the [Makatao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makatao_people \"Makatao people\") tribe, who inhabited the area at the time of European and Hoklo settlement. The Makatao is considered by some to be part of the [Siraya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siraya_people \"Siraya people\") tribe.[[7]](#cite_note-11)\n\nDuring the [Dutch colonization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Formosa \"Dutch Formosa\") of southern Taiwan, the area was known as *Tancoia* to Europeans for a period of about three decades.[*[specify](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources \"Wikipedia:Citing sources\")*] In 1662, the Dutch were expelled by the [Kingdom of Tungning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Tungning \"Kingdom of Tungning\"), founded by Ming loyalists of [Koxinga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koxinga \"Koxinga\"). His son, [Zheng Jing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zheng_Jing \"Zheng Jing\"), renamed the village *Banlian-chiu* (Chinese: 萬年州; Pe̍h-ōe-jī: *Bān-liân-chiu*; lit. 'ten-thousand-year state (*[zhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhou_(country_subdivision) \"Zhou (country subdivision)\")*)') in 1664.\n\nThe name of \"Takau\" was restored in the late 1670s, when the town expanded drastically with immigrants from [mainland China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_China \"Mainland China\") and was kept through Taiwan's cession to the Japanese Empire in 1895. In his 1903 general history of Taiwan, US Consul to Formosa [James W. Davidson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_W._Davidson \"James W. Davidson\") relates that \"Takow\" was already a well-known name in English.[[8]](#cite_note-12) In 1920, the name was changed to *Takao* ([Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_language \"Japanese language\"): 高雄; after [Takao (Kyoto)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takao_(Kyoto)?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Takao (Kyoto) (page does not exist)\") [[ja](https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/高雄%20(京都市) \"ja:高雄 (京都市)\")], a place in [Ukyō Ward, Kyoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukyō-ku,_Kyoto \"Ukyō-ku, Kyoto\")) and administered the area under [Takao Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takao_Prefecture \"Takao Prefecture\"). While the new name had quite a different surface meaning, its pronunciation in Japanese sounded more or less the same as the old name spoken in [Hokkien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hokkien \"Hokkien\").\n\nAfter Taiwan was ceded to the Republic of China, the Chinese characters did not change, but adapted to Mandarin pronunciation, thus the official romanization became Kaohsiung ([pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin \"Pinyin\"): *Gāoxióng*; [Wade–Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wade–Giles \"Wade–Giles\"): *Kao¹-hsiung²*), derived from the [Wade–Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wade–Giles \"Wade–Giles\") romanization of the [Mandarin Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese \"Mandarin Chinese\") pronunciation for 高雄.\n\nThe name *Takau* remains the official name of the city in [Austronesian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formosan_languages \"Formosan languages\") of Taiwan such as [Rukai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rukai_language \"Rukai language\"), although these are not widely spoken in the city. The name also remains popular locally in the naming of businesses, associations, and events.\n\n## History\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Harbor_Takao.jpg/250px-Harbor_Takao.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nThe written history of Kaohsiung can be traced back to the early 17th century, through archaeological studies have found signs of human activity in the region from as long as 7,000 years ago. Prior to the 17th century, the region was inhabited by the [Makatao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makatao_people \"Makatao people\") people of the [Siraya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siraya_people \"Siraya people\") tribe, who settled on what they named Takau Isle (translated to 打狗嶼 by Ming Chinese explorers); \"Takau\" meaning \"bamboo forest\" in the aboriginal language.[[9]](#cite_note-13)\n\n### Early history\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Makatao.jpg/250px-Makatao.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nThe earliest evidence of human activity in the Kaohsiung area dates back to roughly 4,700–5,200 years ago. Most of the discovered remnants were located in the hills surrounding [Kaohsiung Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Kaohsiung \"Port of Kaohsiung\"). Artifacts were found at [Shoushan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoushan_(Kaohsiung) \"Shoushan (Kaohsiung)\"), Longquan Temple, Taoziyuan, [Zuoying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuoying_District \"Zuoying District\"), Houjing, Fudingjin and [Fengbitou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengbitou_Archaeological_Site \"Fengbitou Archaeological Site\"). The prehistoric [Dapenkeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dabenkeng_culture \"Dabenkeng culture\"), [Niuchouzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niuchouzi_Culture \"Niuchouzi Culture\"), [Dahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tahu_Culture \"Tahu Culture\"), and [Niaosong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Niaosung_Culture \"Niaosung Culture\") civilizations were known to inhabit the region. Studies of the prehistoric ruins at Longquan Temple have shown that that civilization occurred at roughly the same times as the beginnings of the aboriginal [Makatao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makatao_people \"Makatao people\") civilization, suggesting a possible origin for the latter. Unlike some other archaeological sites in the area, the Longquan Temple ruins are relatively well preserved. Prehistoric artifacts discovered have suggested that the ancient Kaohsiung Harbor was originally a lagoon, with early civilizations functioning primarily as [Hunter-gatherer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter-gatherer \"Hunter-gatherer\") societies. Some [agricultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture \"Agriculture\") tools have also been discovered, suggesting that some agricultural activity was also present.\n\nThe first Chinese records of the region were written in 1603 by [Chen Di](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chen_Di \"Chen Di\"), a member of [Ming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty \"Ming dynasty\") admiral Shen You-rong's expedition to rid the waters around Taiwan and [Penghu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penghu \"Penghu\") of pirates. In his report on the \"Eastern Barbarian Lands\" (Dong Fan Ji), Chen Di referred to a Ta-kau Isle:\n\nIt is unknown when the barbarians ([Taiwanese aborigines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwanese_aborigines \"Taiwanese aborigines\")) arose on this island in the ocean beyond [Penghu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penghu \"Penghu\"), but they are present at Keeong Harbor (nowaday's [Budai, Chiayi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budai,_Chiayi \"Budai, Chiayi\")), the bay of Galaw ([Anping, Tainan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anping,_Tainan \"Anping, Tainan\")), Laydwawan ([Tainan City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tainan_City \"Tainan City\")), Yaw Harbor ([Cheting, Kaohsiung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheting_District \"Cheting District\")), Takau Isle (Kaohsiung City), Little Tamsui ([Donggang, Pingtung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donggang,_Pingtung \"Donggang, Pingtung\")), Siangkeykaw ([Puzi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puzi \"Puzi\"), Chiayi), Gali forest ([Jiali District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiali_District \"Jiali District\"), Tainan), the village of Sabah ([Tamsui, Taipei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamsui,_Taipei \"Tamsui, Taipei\")), and Dwabangkang ([Bali, New Taipei City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bali_District \"Bali District\")).\n\n### Dutch Formosa\n\nTaiwan became a [Dutch colony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Formosa \"Dutch Formosa\") in 1624, after the [Dutch East India Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_East_India_Company \"Dutch East India Company\") was ejected from [Penghu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penghu \"Penghu\") by [Ming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty \"Ming dynasty\") forces. At the time, Takau was already one of the most important fishing ports in southern Taiwan. The Dutch named the place *Tankoya*, and the harbor *Tancoia*. The Dutch missionary [François Valentijn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/François_Valentyn \"François Valentyn\") named Takau Mountain \"Ape Berg\", a name that would find its way onto European navigational charts well into the 18th century. *Tankoia* was located north of Ape's Hill and a few hours south from Tayouan (modern-day [Anping, Tainan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anping_District \"Anping District\")) by sail.[[10]](#cite_note-14) At the time, a wide shallow bay existed there, sufficient for small vessels. However, constant [silting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siltation \"Siltation\") changed the coastline.\n\nDuring this time, Taiwan was divided into five administrative districts, with Takau belonging to the southernmost district. In 1630, the first large scale immigration of [Han Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese \"Han Chinese\") to Taiwan began due to famine in [Fujian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fujian \"Fujian\"), with merchants and traders from China seeking to purchase hunting licenses from the Dutch or hide out in aboriginal villages to escape authorities in China.\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/da/Takow_harbour%2C_Formosa_by_John_Thomson_Wellcome_L0056431_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Takow_harbour%2C_Formosa_by_John_Thomson_Wellcome_L0056431_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n### Qing dynasty\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/2010_07_%E9%B3%B3%E5%B1%B1%E7%B8%A3%E8%88%8A%E5%9F%8E%E5%8D%97%E9%96%80%E5%9C%93%E7%92%B0.jpg/250px-2010_07_%E9%B3%B3%E5%B1%B1%E7%B8%A3%E8%88%8A%E5%9F%8E%E5%8D%97%E9%96%80%E5%9C%93%E7%92%B0.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nIn 1684, the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty \"Qing dynasty\") annexed [Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_under_Qing_rule \"Taiwan under Qing rule\") and renamed the town Fongshan County ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language \"Chinese language\"): 鳳山縣; [pinyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinyin \"Pinyin\"): *Fèngshān Xiàn*), considering it a part of [Taiwan Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Prefecture \"Taiwan Prefecture\"). It was first opened as a port during the 1680s and subsequently prospered fairly for generations.[[11]](#cite_note-15)\n\n### Japanese rule\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Kaohsiung_Station_and_the_city_2017-10-30.jpg/250px-Kaohsiung_Station_and_the_city_2017-10-30.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nIn 1895, Taiwan was ceded to [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan \"Empire of Japan\") as part of the [Treaty of Shimonoseki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Shimonoseki \"Treaty of Shimonoseki\"). Administrative control of the city was moved from New Fongshan Castle to the Fongshan Sub-District of *Tainan Chō* (臺南廳). In November 1901, twenty *chō* were established in total; *Hōzan Chō* (鳳山廳) was established nearby. In 1909, Hōzan Chō was abolished, and Takow was merged into Tainan Chō.\n\nIn 1920, during the tenure of 8th Governor-General [Den Kenjirō](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Den_Kenjirō \"Den Kenjirō\"), districts were abolished in favor of prefectures. Thus the city was administered as Takao City (高雄市, *Takao-shi*) under [Takao Prefecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takao_Prefecture \"Takao Prefecture\").\n\nThe Japanese developed Takao, especially the harbor that became the foundation of Kaohsiung to be a port city. Takao was then systematically modernized and connected to the end of [North-South Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_line_(Taiwan) \"West Coast line (Taiwan)\"). Forming a north–south regional economic corridor from Taipei to Kaohsiung in the 1930s, Japan's Southward Policy set Kaohsiung to become an industrial center.[[12]](#cite_note-16) [Kaohsiung Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Kaohsiung \"Port of Kaohsiung\") was also developed starting from 1894. The city center was relocated several times during the period due to the government's development strategy.[[13]](#cite_note-17) Development was initially centered on *Ki-au* ([Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_language \"Chinese language\"): [旗後](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/旗後 \"zh:旗後\"); [Pe̍h-ōe-jī](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pe̍h-ōe-jī \"Pe̍h-ōe-jī\"): *Kî-āu*) region but the government began laying railways, upgrading the harbor, and passing new urban plans. New industries such as refinery, machinery, shipbuilding and cementing were also introduced.\n\nBefore and during [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II \"World War II\") it handled a growing share of Taiwan's agricultural exports to Japan, and was also a major base for Japan's campaigns in Southeast Asia and the Pacific. Extremely ambitious plans for the construction of a massive modern port were drawn up. Toward the end of the war, the Japanese promoted some industrial development at Kaohsiung, establishing an aluminum industry based on the abundant [hydroelectric power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydroelectricity \"Hydroelectricity\") produced by the [Sun Moon Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun_Moon_Lake \"Sun Moon Lake\") project in the mountains.\n\nThe city was heavily bombed by [Task Force 38](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Task_Force_38 \"Task Force 38\") and [FEAF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_East_Air_Force_(United_States) \"Far East Air Force (United States)\") during World War II between 1944 and 1945.[[14]](#cite_note-18)\n\n### Republic of China\n\nAfter control of Taiwan was [handed over from Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Taiwan_since_1945 \"History of Taiwan since 1945\") to the government of the [Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China \"Republic of China\") on 25 October 1945, Kaohsiung City and [Kaohsiung County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_County \"Kaohsiung County\") were established as a [provincial city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_city_(Taiwan) \"Provincial city (Taiwan)\") and a [county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_(Taiwan) \"County (Taiwan)\") of [Taiwan Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_(Republic_of_China_province) \"Taiwan (Republic of China province)\") respectively on 25 December 1945. The official romanization of the name came to be \"Kaohsiung\", based on the [Wade–Giles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wade–Giles \"Wade–Giles\") romanization of the [Mandarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese \"Mandarin Chinese\") reading of the [kanji](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanji \"Kanji\") name.[[15]](#cite_note-19) Kaohsiung City then consisted of 10 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_(Taiwan) \"District (Taiwan)\"), which were [Gushan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gushan_District \"Gushan District\"), [Lianya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingya_District \"Lingya District\") (renamed \"Lingya\" in 1952), [Nanzih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanzih_District \"Nanzih District\"), [Cianjin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qianjin_District,_Kaohsiung \"Qianjin District, Kaohsiung\"), [Cianjhen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cianjhen_District \"Cianjhen District\"), [Cijin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cijin_District,_Kaohsiung \"Cijin District, Kaohsiung\"), [Sanmin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanmin_District \"Sanmin District\"), [Sinsing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinsing_District,_Kaohsiung \"Sinsing District, Kaohsiung\"), [Yancheng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yancheng_District,_Kaohsiung \"Yancheng District, Kaohsiung\"), and [Zuoying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuoying_District \"Zuoying District\").\n\nDuring this time, Kaohsiung developed rapidly. The port, badly damaged in [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II \"World War II\"), was restored. It also became a fishing port for boats sailing to Filipino and Indonesian waters. Largely because of its climate, Kaohsiung overtook [Keelung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keelung \"Keelung\") as Taiwan's major port. Kaohsiung also surpassed Tainan to become the second largest city of Taiwan in the late 1970s and Kaohsiung City was upgraded from a [provincial city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provincial_city_(Taiwan) \"Provincial city (Taiwan)\") to [special municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_municipality_(Taiwan) \"Special municipality (Taiwan)\") on 1 July 1979, by the [Executive Yuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Yuan \"Executive Yuan\") with a total of 11 districts.[[16]](#cite_note-20) The additional district is [Siaogang District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siaogang_District \"Siaogang District\"), which was annexed from Siaogang Township of [Kaohsiung County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_County \"Kaohsiung County\").\n\nThe [Kaohsiung Incident](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Incident \"Kaohsiung Incident\"), where the government suppressed a commemoration of International [Human Rights Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Rights_Day \"Human Rights Day\"), occurred on 10 December 1979. Since then, Kaohsiung gradually grew into a political center of the [Pan-Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Green_Coalition \"Pan-Green Coalition\") population of Taiwan, in opposition to [Taipei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taipei \"Taipei\") where the majority population is [Kuomintang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang \"Kuomintang\") supporters.\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Kaohsiung-Tainan_before_and_after_2010.svg/250px-Kaohsiung-Tainan_before_and_after_2010.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nOn 25 December 2010, Kaohsiung City merged with [Kaohsiung County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_County \"Kaohsiung County\") to form a larger [special municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_municipality_(Taiwan) \"Special municipality (Taiwan)\") with administrative centers in [Lingya District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingya_District \"Lingya District\") and [Fongshan District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fongshan_District \"Fongshan District\").[[17]](#cite_note-21)\n\nOn 31 July 2014, a series of [gas explosions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Kaohsiung_gas_explosions \"2014 Kaohsiung gas explosions\") occurred in the [Cianjhen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cianjhen_District \"Cianjhen District\") and [Lingya Districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingya_District \"Lingya District\") of the city, killing 31 and injuring more than 300. Five roads were destroyed in an area of nearly 20 km2 (7.7 sq mi) near the city center. It was the largest gas explosion in Taiwan's modern history.[[18]](#cite_note-22)\n\n## Geography\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/JadeMountainSouthernPeak.jpg/250px-JadeMountainSouthernPeak.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nThe city sits on the southwestern coast of Taiwan facing the [Taiwan Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Strait \"Taiwan Strait\"), bordering [Tainan City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tainan_City \"Tainan City\") to the northwest, [Chiayi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiayi \"Chiayi\") and [Nantou County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nantou_County \"Nantou County\") to the north, [Taitung County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taitung_County \"Taitung County\") to its east and [Pingtung County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingtung_County \"Pingtung County\") to the south and southeast. The downtown areas are centered on Kaohsiung Harbor with [Cijin Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cijin_District,_Kaohsiung \"Cijin District, Kaohsiung\") on the other side of the harbor acting as a natural [breakwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breakwater_(structure) \"Breakwater (structure)\"). The [Love River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_River \"Love River\") (Ai River) flows into the harbor through the Old City and downtown. Zuoying Military Harbor lies to the north of Kaohsiung Harbor and the city center. Kaohsiung's natural landmarks include [Ape Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoushan_(Kaohsiung) \"Shoushan (Kaohsiung)\") and [Mount Banping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Banping \"Mount Banping\").\n\n### Climate\n\n| Kaohsiung |\n| --- |\n| Climate chart ([explanation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template:Climate_chart/How_to_read_a_climate_chart \"Template:Climate chart/How to | |\n| █ Precipitation totals in inches | | | | | | | | | | | |\n\nLocated about a degree south of the [Tropic of Cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropic_of_Cancer \"Tropic of Cancer\"), Kaohsiung has a [tropical savanna climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_savanna_climate \"Tropical savanna climate\") ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Köppen_climate_classification \"Köppen climate classification\") *Aw*), near to a [tropical monsoon climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_monsoon_climate \"Tropical monsoon climate\") ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Köppen_climate_classification \"Köppen climate classification\") *Am*)[[19]](#cite_note-23) with monthly mean temperatures between 20 and 29 °C (68 and 84 °F) and relative humidity ranging between 71 and 81%.\n\nKaohsiung's warm climate is very much dictated by its low latitude and its exposure to warm sea temperatures year-round, with the [Kuroshio Current](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuroshio_Current \"Kuroshio Current\") passing by the coast of southern Taiwan,[[20]](#cite_note-24) and the [Central Mountain Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Mountain_Range \"Central Mountain Range\") on the northeast blocking out the cool northeastern winds during the winter. The city, therefore, has a noticeably warmer climate than nearby cities located at similar latitudes such as [Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong \"Hong Kong\"), [Guangzhou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou \"Guangzhou\") as well as various cities further south in northern [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam \"Vietnam\"), such as [Hanoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanoi \"Hanoi\"). Although the climate is classified as tropical, Kaohsiung has a defined cooler season unlike most other cities in Asia classified with this climate but located closer to the equator such as [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore \"Singapore\") or [Manila](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manila \"Manila\"). Daily maximum temperature typically exceeds 30 °C (86 °F) during the warmer season (April to November) and 25 °C (77 °F) during the cooler season (December to March), with the exception when cold fronts strike during the winter months, when the daily mean temperature of the city can drop between 10 and 12 °C depending on the strength of the cold front. Also, besides the high temperatures occurring during the usual summer months, daytime temperatures of inland districts of the city can often exceed 33 °C (91 °F) from mid-March to late April before the onset of the [monsoon season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsoon_season \"Monsoon season\"), with clear skies and southwesterly airflows. Average annual rainfall is around 1,885 mm (74.2 in), focused primarily from June to August. At more than 2,210 hours of bright sunshine, the city is one of the sunniest areas in Taiwan.[[21]](#cite_note-CWB-25)\n\nThe sea temperature of [Kaohsiung Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Harbor \"Kaohsiung Harbor\") remains above 22 °C (72 °F) year-round,[[22]](#cite_note-26) the second highest of Southern Taiwan after [Liuqiu Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liuqiu_Island \"Liuqiu Island\").[[23]](#cite_note-27) According to recent records, the average temperature of the city has risen around 1 degree Celsius over the past three decades, from about 24.2 °C (75.6 °F) in 1983 to around 25.2 °C (77.4 °F) by 2012.\n\n| Climate data for Kaohsiung City (1991–2020 normals, extremes 1931–present） | | | | | | | | | | | | | Retrieved 15 July 2026. | | |\n\nAs of August 2025, Kaohsiung has a population of 2,722,984 people, making it the third-largest city after [New Taipei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Taipei_City \"New Taipei City\") and [Taichung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taichung \"Taichung\"), and a population density of 922.47 people per square kilometer.[[3]](#cite_note-sr-6) Within the city, [Fongshan District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fongshan_District \"Fongshan District\") is the most populated district with a population of 354,455 people, while [Sinsing District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinsing_District,_Kaohsiung \"Sinsing District, Kaohsiung\") is the most densely populated district with a population density of 25,052 people per square kilometer.\n\n### Ethnic composition\n\n|  |  |\n| --- | --- |\n| [icon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **needs [more citations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability \"Wikipedia:Verifiability\")**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Kaohsiung \"Special:EditPage/Kaohsiung\") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners \"Help:Referencing for beginners\"). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence \"Wikipedia:Verifiability\"). *(August 2020)* *([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal \"Help:Maintenance template removal\"))* |\n\n![icon](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/60px-Question_book-new.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n#### Han Chinese\n\nAs in most Taiwanese cities or counties, the majority of the population are [Han Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_Chinese \"Han Chinese\"). The Chinese are divided into 3 subgroups: [Hoklo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoklo_people \"Hoklo people\"), [Hakka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakka \"Hakka\"), and [Waishengren](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waishengren \"Waishengren\"). The Hoklo and Waishengren mostly live in flatland townships and the city centre, while the majority of the Hakka population lives in the suburbs or rural townships of the northeastern hills.\n\n#### Indigenous peoples\n\nThe [indigenous peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwanese_indigenous_peoples \"Taiwanese indigenous peoples\") of Kaohsiung, who belong to various ethnic groups that speak languages belonging to the [Austronesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_languages \"Austronesian languages\") language family, live mostly in the [mountain indigenous district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_(Taiwan) \"District (Taiwan)\") such as [Taoyuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoyuan_District,_Kaohsiung \"Taoyuan District, Kaohsiung\") or [Namasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namasia \"Namasia\"). The main indigenous groups in the city include the [Bunun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bunun_people \"Bunun people\"), [Rukai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rukai_people \"Rukai people\"), [Saaroa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saaroa_people \"Saaroa people\") and the [Kanakanavu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kanakanavu_people \"Kanakanavu people\").\n\n#### Other ethnicities\n\nAs of December 2010, Kaohsiung hosts around 21,000 foreign spouses. Around 12,353 are Mainland Chinese, 4,244 are Vietnamese, around 800 are Japanese and Indonesians, and around 4,000 are other Asians or foreigners from Europe or America.\n\nAs of April 2013, Kaohsiung hosts 35,074 [foreign workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_workers \"Foreign workers\") who mainly work as factory workers or foreign maids (not including foreign specialists such as teachers and other professionals). About half of them are [Indonesians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Overseas_Indonesian \"Overseas Indonesian\"), with the other half being workers from other [Southeast Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Asian \"Southeast Asian\") countries, mainly from [Vietnam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnam \"Vietnam\"), the [Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philippines \"Philippines\") or [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand \"Thailand\").\n\n## Economy\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/%E9%AB%98%E9%9B%84%E6%B8%AF_Kaohsiung_Harbor_%E5%85%A8%E8%B2%8C_%2862571253%29.jpeg/250px-%E9%AB%98%E9%9B%84%E6%B8%AF_Kaohsiung_Harbor_%E5%85%A8%E8%B2%8C_%2862571253%29.jpeg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/65/Downtown_Kaohsiung_Skyline_20211017.jpg/250px-Downtown_Kaohsiung_Skyline_20211017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nKaohsiung is a major international port and industrial city in the southwest of Taiwan. As an exporting center, Kaohsiung serves the agricultural interior of southern Taiwan, as well as the mountains of the southeast. Major [raw material](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raw_material \"Raw material\") exports include rice, sugar, bananas, [pineapples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pineapple \"Pineapple\"), peanuts (groundnuts) and [citrus fruits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citrus_fruit \"Citrus fruit\"). The 2,200 ha (5,400-acre) [Linhai Industrial Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linhai_Industrial_Park \"Linhai Industrial Park\"), on the waterfront, was completed in the mid-1970s and includes a [steel mill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel_mill \"Steel mill\"), [shipyard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shipyard \"Shipyard\"), [petrochemical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrochemical \"Petrochemical\") complex, and other industries. The city has an [oil refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalin_Refinery \"Dalin Refinery\"), aluminum and cement works, [fertilizer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertilizer \"Fertilizer\") factories, sugar refineries, brick and tile works, canning factories, salt-manufacturing factories, and [papermaking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papermaking \"Papermaking\") plants. Designated an [export-processing zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Export-processing_zone \"Export-processing zone\") in the late 1970s, Kaohsiung also attracted foreign investment to process locally purchased raw materials for export.\n\nIn 2020, Kaohsiung's [land reclamation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reclamation \"Land reclamation\") project in the Port of Kaohsiung was completed, equivalent to 16 of [Taipei's Daan Forest Parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daan_Forest_Park \"Daan Forest Park\").[[28]](#cite_note-32)\n\nThe Kaohsiung Harbor Bureau plans to buy 49 hectares of the reclaimed land to establish a solar energy industrial district that would be in the harbor's free trade zone.[[29]](#cite_note-33)\n\nThe gross domestic product (GDP) in nominal terms of Kaohsiung City is estimated to be around US$45 billion, and US$90 billion for the metropolitan region. As of 2008[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kaohsiung&action=edit), the GDP per capita in nominal terms was approximately US$24,000.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nDespite early success and heavy governmental investment, the city suffers from the economic [North–South divide in Taiwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North–South_divide_in_Taiwan \"North–South divide in Taiwan\"), which continues to be the center of political debate.[[30]](#cite_note-34) There has been public aims to shift the local economy towards tourism and [cultural industries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_industry \"Cultural industry\"), with projects such as [Pier-2 Art Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier-2_Art_Center \"Pier-2 Art Center\"), [National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Kaohsiung_Center_for_the_Arts \"National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts\") and [Kaohsiung Music Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Music_Center \"Kaohsiung Music Center\").\n\n### Agriculture\n\nThe main agricultural crops in Kaohsiung are vegetables, fruits and rice with a total [arable land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arable_land \"Arable land\") of 473 km2, which accounts to 16% of the total area of the municipality. Kaohsiung has the highest production of [guava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guava \"Guava\"), [jujube](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jujube \"Jujube\") and [lychee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lychee \"Lychee\") in Taiwan. The main [livestock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livestock \"Livestock\") are chicken, [dairy cattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_cattle \"Dairy cattle\"), [deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deer \"Deer\"), duck, goose, pigs and sheep. The total annual agricultural income in Kaohsiung is NT$24.15 billion.[[31]](#cite_note-35)\n\n### Future investment\n\n|\n|  |\nInvestment inflow of returned-Taiwanese merchandisers from China due to [US-China trade war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US-China_trade_war \"US-China trade war\").\n\n|  | Numbers of merchants | Investment amounts of money in billions NTD | Jobs-created |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| [Taoyuan City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoyuan_City \"Taoyuan City\") | 39 | 154.0 | 15000 |\n| [Taichung City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taichung_City \"Taichung City\") | 35 | 133.8 | 10000 |\n| Kaohsiung City | 20 | 146.0 | 10000 |\n| [Tainan City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tainan_City \"Tainan City\") | 23 | 123.0 | 7500 |\n| [New Taipei City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Taipei_City \"New Taipei City\") | 9 | 21.0 | 2650 |\n| [Taipei City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taipei_City \"Taipei City\") | 3 | 4.0 | 600 |\n| **Source:**[[32]](#cite_note-36) | | | |\n\n## Culture\n\n### Tourism\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/02/Kaohsiung_Tuntex_Sky_Tower.jpg/250px-Kaohsiung_Tuntex_Sky_Tower.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nMain landmarks of Kaohsiung city include the [85 Sky Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/85_Sky_Tower \"85 Sky Tower\"), the [Ferris wheel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferris_wheel \"Ferris wheel\") of the Kaohsiung [Dream Mall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dream_Mall \"Dream Mall\"), the [Kaohsiung Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Arena \"Kaohsiung Arena\") and [Port of Kaohsiung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Kaohsiung \"Port of Kaohsiung\"). The newly developed city is also known for having a large number of [shopping streets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shopping_streets \"Shopping streets\"), organized [night markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_markets \"Night markets\") and newly developed leisure parks such as the [Pier-2 Art Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pier-2_Art_Center \"Pier-2 Art Center\"), [E-DA Theme Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-DA_Theme_Park \"E-DA Theme Park\"), [Metropolitan Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Metropolitan_Park \"Kaohsiung Metropolitan Park\"), the [Kaohsiung Museum of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Museum_of_Fine_Arts \"Kaohsiung Museum of Fine Arts\"), the [National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Kaohsiung_Center_for_the_Arts \"National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts\") and [SKM Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SKM_Park \"SKM Park\").\n\nNatural attractions of the city include [Shoushan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoushan_(Kaohsiung) \"Shoushan (Kaohsiung)\") (Monkey mountain), the [Love River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_River \"Love River\"), [Cijin Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cijin_District \"Cijin District\"), [Sizihwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sizihwan \"Sizihwan\"), the [Dapingding Tropical Botanical Garden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dapingding_Tropical_Botanical_Garden \"Dapingding Tropical Botanical Garden\") and [Yushan National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yushan_National_Park \"Yushan National Park\") at the northeastern tip of the city. The city also features various historical attractions such as the [Old City of Zuoying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_City_of_Zuoying \"Old City of Zuoying\"), a historical town built during the early 17th century, the [Former British Consulate at Takao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_British_Consulate_at_Takao \"Former British Consulate at Takao\") built during the late 19th century, and various sugar and crop factories built under [Japanese rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_under_Japanese_rule \"Taiwan under Japanese rule\").\n\n#### Natural attractions\n\nKaohsiung city includes a wide range of different natural attractions due to its large size and geographical variation, as it is bordered by the [Central Mountain Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Mountain_Range \"Central Mountain Range\") in the northeast and the warm [South China Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_China_Sea \"South China Sea\") to the west and southwest. The year-round warm climate allows [coral reefs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coral_reefs \"Coral reefs\") to grow along the coasts around [Kaohsiung Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Harbor \"Kaohsiung Harbor\"), with [Shoushan Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shoushan_(Kaohsiung) \"Shoushan (Kaohsiung)\") being a small mountain completely made up of coral reefs and [calcium carbonate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calcium_carbonate \"Calcium carbonate\"), while the mountainous districts in the northeast include Taiwan's highest mountain, [Yushan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yushan_(mountain) \"Yushan (mountain)\"). Other notable natural attractions include the [Mount Banping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Banping \"Mount Banping\"), [Lotus Pond](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lotus_Pond,_Kaohsiung \"Lotus Pond, Kaohsiung\"), and [Dongsha Atoll National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dongsha_Atoll_National_Park \"Dongsha Atoll National Park\"), which is currently inaccessible by the public due to military occupation.\n\n#### Historical sites\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/27/Former_British_Consulate_at_Takao.jpg/250px-Former_British_Consulate_at_Takao.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nA large number of historical sites and monuments were left in the city after the colonization of the Dutch in the 17th century, the Qing dynasty during the 18th and 19th century and the Japanese empire from the late 19th century to the mid 20th century. The city government has protected various sites and monuments from further damage and many have been opened to the public since the early 1980s. Notable historical sites include the [Cemetery of Zhenghaijun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cemetery_of_Zhenghaijun \"Cemetery of Zhenghaijun\"), [Fengshan Longshan Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengshan_Longshan_Temple \"Fengshan Longshan Temple\"), [Former British Consulate at Takao](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_British_Consulate_at_Takao \"Former British Consulate at Takao\"), [Former Dinglinzihbian Police Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Dinglinzihbian_Police_Station \"Former Dinglinzihbian Police Station\"), [Meinong Cultural and Creative Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meinong_Cultural_and_Creative_Center \"Meinong Cultural and Creative Center\"), [Former Sanhe Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Sanhe_Bank \"Former Sanhe Bank\"), and the [Kaohsiung Lighthouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Lighthouse \"Kaohsiung Lighthouse\"), one of the oldest lighthouses of the city.\n\n#### Museums\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Confucius_temple_Kaohsiung_amk.jpg/250px-Confucius_temple_Kaohsiung_amk.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nKaohsiung is home to many museums, including the [Chung Li-ho Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chung_Li-ho_Museum \"Chung Li-ho Museum\"), [Cijin Shell Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cijin_Shell_Museum \"Cijin Shell Museum\"), [Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fo_Guang_Shan_Buddha_Museum \"Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum\"), [Jiasian Petrified Fossil Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiasian_Petrified_Fossil_Museum \"Jiasian Petrified Fossil Museum\"), [Kaohsiung Astronomical Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Astronomical_Museum \"Kaohsiung Astronomical Museum\"), [Kaohsiung Hakka Cultural Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Hakka_Cultural_Museum \"Kaohsiung Hakka Cultural Museum\"), [Kaohsiung Harbor Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Harbor_Museum \"Kaohsiung Harbor Museum\"), [Kaohsiung Museum of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Museum_of_Fine_Arts \"Kaohsiung Museum of Fine Arts\"), [Kaohsiung Museum of History](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Museum_of_History \"Kaohsiung Museum of History\"), [Kaohsiung Museum of Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Museum_of_Labor \"Kaohsiung Museum of Labor\"), [Kaohsiung Vision Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Vision_Museum \"Kaohsiung Vision Museum\"), [Meinong Hakka Culture Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meinong_Hakka_Culture_Museum \"Meinong Hakka Culture Museum\"), [National Science and Technology Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Science_and_Technology_Museum \"National Science and Technology Museum\"), [Republic of China Air Force Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Air_Force_Museum \"Republic of China Air Force Museum\"), [Soya-Mixed Meat Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soya-Mixed_Meat_Museum \"Soya-Mixed Meat Museum\"), [Taiwan Pineapple Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Pineapple_Museum \"Taiwan Pineapple Museum\"), [Taiwan Sugar Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Sugar_Museum_(Kaohsiung) \"Taiwan Sugar Museum (Kaohsiung)\"), [Takao Railway Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Takao_Railway_Museum \"Takao Railway Museum\"), [Xiaolin Pingpu Cultural Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xiaolin_Pingpu_Cultural_Museum \"Xiaolin Pingpu Cultural Museum\") and [YM Museum of Marine Exploration Kaohsiung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YM_Museum_of_Marine_Exploration_Kaohsiung \"YM Museum of Marine Exploration Kaohsiung\").\n\n#### Parks and zoos\n\nAs the largest municipality in Taiwan, Kaohsiung has a number of newly built leisure areas and parks. Notable parks or pavilions in the city include the [Central Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park_(Kaohsiung) \"Central Park (Kaohsiung)\"), [Siaogangshan Skywalk Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siaogangshan_Skywalk_Park \"Siaogangshan Skywalk Park\"), [Fo Guang Shan Monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fo_Guang_Shan_Monastery \"Fo Guang Shan Monastery\"), the [Dragon and Tiger Pagodas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dragon_and_Tiger_Pagodas \"Dragon and Tiger Pagodas\"), [Spring and Autumn Pavilions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_and_Autumn_Pavilions \"Spring and Autumn Pavilions\"), the [Love Pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Pier \"Love Pier\"), [Singuang Ferry Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singuang_Ferry_Wharf \"Singuang Ferry Wharf\") and [Kaohsiung Fisherman's Wharf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Fisherman's_Wharf \"Kaohsiung Fisherman's Wharf\"). Notable zoo in the city includes the [Kaohsiung City Shousan Zoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_City_Shousan_Zoo?action=edit&redlink=1 \"Kaohsiung City Shousan Zoo (page does not exist)\").[[33]](#cite_note-37)\n\n#### Other\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/57/Liouho-Night-Market-Kaohsiung.jpg/250px-Liouho-Night-Market-Kaohsiung.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nKaohsiung is home to many [night markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Night_markets_in_Taiwan \"Night markets in Taiwan\"), such as [Jin-Zuan Night Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jin-Zuan_Night_Market \"Jin-Zuan Night Market\"), [Kaisyuan Night Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaisyuan_Night_Market \"Kaisyuan Night Market\"), [Liuhe Night Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liuhe_Night_Market \"Liuhe Night Market\"), [Rueifong Night Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rueifong_Night_Market \"Rueifong Night Market\") and [Zhonghua Street Night Market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhonghua_Street_Night_Market \"Zhonghua Street Night Market\"). Other attractions include the [Cijin Tianhou Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cijin_Tianhou_Temple \"Cijin Tianhou Temple\"), [Dome of Light of Kaohsiung MRT's Formosa Boulevard Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formosa_Boulevard_metro_station \"Formosa Boulevard metro station\"), the [Kaohsiung Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Mosque \"Kaohsiung Mosque\") and the [Tower of Light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tower_of_Light \"Tower of Light\") of [Sanmin District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanmin_District \"Sanmin District\").\n\n[Traditional \"wet\" markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wet_market \"Wet market\") have long been the source of meat, fish, and produce for many residents. With the arrival of Western-style [supermarkets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supermarkets \"Supermarkets\") in the 1980s and 1990s, such markets have encountered fierce competition. In 1989, the global leader in hypermarkets, [Carrefour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carrefour \"Carrefour\"), entered Asia, opening its first store in Kaohsiung. Due to the success of its Taiwan operation, the French retailer expanded throughout the country and Asia. Jean-Luc Chéreau, the general manager in Taiwan from 1993 to 1999, used this newfound understanding of Chinese culture and ways of doing business with Chinese customers to lead its China expansion starting in 1999.[[34]](#cite_note-38) As of February 2020, Carrefour has opened 137 hypermarkets and supermarkets in Taiwan.[[35]](#cite_note-39) Despite the fierce competition from \"Westernized\" supermarkets, Taiwan's traditional markets and mom-and-pop stores remain \"one of the most popular retail formats for many Asian families when they purchase daily food items and basic household goods.\"[[36]](#cite_note-40)\n\n### Languages\n\nThe majority of those living in Kaohsiung can communicate in both [Taiwanese Hokkien](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwanese_Hokkien \"Taiwanese Hokkien\") and [Mandarin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standard_Chinese \"Standard Chinese\"). Some of the elderly who grew up during the Japanese colonization of Taiwan can communicate in Japanese, while most of the younger population have basic English skills.\n\nSince the spread of Standard Chinese after the [Nationalist Government retreated to Taiwan in 1949](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War \"Chinese Civil War\"), [Hakka Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakka_Chinese \"Hakka Chinese\") and various [Formosan languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formosan_languages \"Formosan languages\") are gradually no longer spoken with the new generation and many Formosan languages are therefore classified as [moribund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moribund_language \"Moribund language\") or [endangered languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endangered_languages \"Endangered languages\") by the United Nations. Nowadays, only elder Hakka people mostly living in [Meinong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meinong_District \"Meinong District\"), [Liouguei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liouguei_District,_Kaohsiung \"Liouguei District, Kaohsiung\"), [Shanlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanlin_District \"Shanlin District\") and [Jiasian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jiasian_District \"Jiasian District\") districts can communicate in Hakka and elder Taiwanese aborigines living mostly in the rural districts of [Namasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namasia_District \"Namasia District\") and [Taoyuan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoyuan_District,_Kaohsiung \"Taoyuan District, Kaohsiung\") can communicate with the aboriginal languages. The Taiwanese government has established special affairs committees for both the Aboriginals and the Hakkas to protect their language, culture, and minority rights.\n\n### Arts\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/FormosaBoulevardStation.JPG/250px-FormosaBoulevardStation.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nThe \"Dome of Light\" in the concourse of [Formosa Boulevard Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formosa_Boulevard_metro_station \"Formosa Boulevard metro station\") of [Kaohsiung MRT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_MRT \"Kaohsiung MRT\") is one of the world's largest public [glass works](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glass_works \"Glass works\") of art.[[37]](#cite_note-41) The city also has the [Urban Spotlight Arcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_Spotlight_Arcade \"Urban Spotlight Arcade\") spanning along the street in [Cianjin District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cianjin_District \"Cianjin District\"). Acknowledged as the largest performance arts center under one roof in the world Weiwuying ([the National Kaohsiung Centre for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Kaohsiung_Center_for_the_Arts \"National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts\")), opened in 2018. The center was designed by [Mecanoo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mecanoo \"Mecanoo\").[[38]](#cite_note-42)\n\n## Religion\n\nThe religious population of Kaohsiung may be divided into five main religious groups: [Buddhists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhists \"Buddhists\"), [Taoists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoists \"Taoists\"), [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim \"Muslim\") and [Christians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christians \"Christians\") ([Catholics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholics \"Catholics\") and [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestants \"Protestants\")). As of 2015[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Kaohsiung&action=edit), Kaohsiung City has 1,481 temples, the second highest in Taiwan after [Tainan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tainan \"Tainan\"). Kaohsiung also has 306 churches.[[40]](#cite_note-44)\n\n### Buddhism\n\nBuddhism is one of the major religions in Taiwan, with over 35% of Taiwan's population identifying as Buddhists.[[41]](#cite_note-45) The same applies to Kaohsiung city. Kaohsiung also hosts the largest [Buddhist temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist_temple \"Buddhist temple\") in Taiwan, the [Fo Guang Shan Monastery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fo_Guang_Shan_Monastery \"Fo Guang Shan Monastery\") with its [Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fo_Guang_Shan_Buddha_Museum \"Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum\"). There are also other famous Buddhist temples such as [Fengshan Longshan Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengshan_Longshan_Temple \"Fengshan Longshan Temple\") and [Hong Fa Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Fa_Temple \"Hong Fa Temple\").\n\n### Taoism\n\nAround 33% of the Taiwanese population are Taoists, making it the second largest religion of Taiwan. Most people who believe in Taoism also ascribe to Buddhism at the same time, as the differences and boundaries between the two religions are not always clear. Many residents of the area also worship the sea goddess known as Tian Shang Sheng Mu (天上聖母) or [Mazu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazu \"Mazu\"), who is variously [syncretized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syncretized \"Syncretized\") as a [Taoist immortal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoist_immortal \"Taoist immortal\") or embodiment of the [bodhisattva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bodhisattva \"Bodhisattva\") [Guanyin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guanyin \"Guanyin\"). Her temple on Cijin Island, [Chi Jin Mazu Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chi_Jin_Mazu_Temple \"Chi Jin Mazu Temple\"), is the oldest in the city, with its original bamboo-and-thatch structure first opened in 1673. The area surrounding it formed the center of the city's early settlement.[[42]](#cite_note-herry-46) There are also other prominent Taoist temples such as [Fengshan Tiangong Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengshan_Tiangong_Temple \"Fengshan Tiangong Temple\"), dedicated to the [Jade Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jade_Emperor \"Jade Emperor\"), [Cih Ji Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cih_Ji_Palace \"Cih Ji Palace\"), dedicated to [Bao Sheng Da Di](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baosheng_Dadi \"Baosheng Dadi\"), [Qing Shui Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zhouzi_Qingshui_Temple \"Zhouzi Qingshui Temple\"), dedicated to [Qing Shui Zu Shi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qingshui_(monk) \"Qingshui (monk)\") and [Gushan Daitian Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gushan_Daitian_Temple \"Gushan Daitian Temple\") dedicated to [Wang Ye worship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wang_Ye_worship \"Wang Ye worship\").\n\n### Christianity\n\nChristianity is a minority religion in Taiwan. It was first brought onto the island when the Dutch and Spanish colonized Taiwan during the 17th century, mostly to the aboriginals. Kaohsiung currently hosts around 56,000 Christians.\n\n### Islam\n\nBesides the majority population of Buddhists and Taoists, Kaohsiung also includes a rather tiny population of [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslims \"Muslims\"). During the [Chinese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War \"Chinese Civil War\"), some 20,000 Muslims, mostly soldiers and civil servants, fled [mainland China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_China \"Mainland China\") with the [Kuomintang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang \"Kuomintang\") government to Taiwan.[[43]](#cite_note-et-47) During the 1980s, another few thousand Muslims from [Myanmar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myanmar \"Myanmar\") and [Thailand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thailand \"Thailand\"), who are mostly descendants of [Nationalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nationalist_Chinese \"Nationalist Chinese\") soldiers who fled [Yunnan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yunnan \"Yunnan\") as a result of the communist takeover, migrated to Taiwan in search of a better life, resulting in an increase of Muslim population within the country.[[43]](#cite_note-et-47) More recently, with the rise of Indonesian workers working in Taiwan, an estimated number of 88,000 Indonesian Muslims currently live in the country, in addition to the existing 53,000 Taiwanese Muslims. Combining all demographics, Taiwan hosts around 140,000 Muslims, with around 25,000 living in Kaohsiung. [Kaohsiung Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Mosque \"Kaohsiung Mosque\") is the largest mosque in Kaohsiung and the main gathering site of Muslims within the city.\n\n![Foguangshan Temple](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9b/Fo_Guang_Shan.jpg/330px-Fo_Guang_Shan.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Cijing Tianhou Temple](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3f/%E6%97%97%E5%BE%8C%E5%A4%A9%E5%90%8E%E5%AE%AE.jpg/330px-%E6%97%97%E5%BE%8C%E5%A4%A9%E5%90%8E%E5%AE%AE.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Holy Rosary Cathedral](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/%E7%8E%AB%E7%91%B0%E8%81%96%E6%AF%8D%E4%B8%BB%E6%95%99%E5%BA%A7%E5%A0%82.JPG/330px-%E7%8E%AB%E7%91%B0%E8%81%96%E6%AF%8D%E4%B8%BB%E6%95%99%E5%BA%A7%E5%A0%82.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Kaohsiung Mosque](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ab/Kaohsiung_Mosque.JPG/330px-Kaohsiung_Mosque.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n## Politics\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/Chen_Chi-mai_election_infobox.jpg/250px-Chen_Chi-mai_election_infobox.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n### Government\n\nSometimes Kaohsiung used to be seen as the political opposite of Taipei. While northern Taiwan leans towards the [Pan-Blue Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Blue_Coalition \"Pan-Blue Coalition\") in the state-level elections, southern Taiwan, including Kaohsiung, leaned towards the [Pan-Green Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan-Green_Coalition \"Pan-Green Coalition\") since the late 1990s. [Frank Hsieh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Hsieh \"Frank Hsieh\") of the [Democratic Progressive Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Progressive_Party \"Democratic Progressive Party\") was reelected twice as Mayor of Kaohsiung, where he was widely credited for transforming the city from an industrial sprawl into an attractive modern metropolis. Hsieh resigned from the office of mayor to take up the office of [Premier of the Republic of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_the_Republic_of_China \"Premier of the Republic of China\") in 2005. The [municipal election, held on 9 December 2006](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2005–06_Taiwanese_local_elections \"2005–06 Taiwanese local elections\"), resulted in a victory for the [Democratic Progressive Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Progressive_Party \"Democratic Progressive Party\")'s candidate [Chen Chu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chen_Chu \"Chen Chu\"), the first elected female mayor of [special municipality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_municipality_of_Taiwan \"Special municipality of Taiwan\") in Taiwan, defeating her [Kuomintang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuomintang \"Kuomintang\") rival and former deputy mayor, [Huang Jun-ying](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huang_Jun-ying \"Huang Jun-ying\"). As of 12 June 2020, the mayor of Kaohsiung City is [Chen Chi-mai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chen_Chi-mai \"Chen Chi-mai\").[[44]](#cite_note-48)\n\n![Kaohsiung City Hall](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Kaohsiung_City_Hall_%28Dec_2010%29.JPG/250px-Kaohsiung_City_Hall_%28Dec_2010%29.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Kaohsiung City Hall](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Fongshan_Administration_Center%2C_Kaohsiung_City_Government_20140720.jpg/250px-Fongshan_Administration_Center%2C_Kaohsiung_City_Government_20140720.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Kaohsiung City Council](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Hall_of_Kaohsiung_City_Council.JPG/250px-Hall_of_Kaohsiung_City_Council.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![Kaohsiung District Court](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/%E8%87%BA%E7%81%A3%E9%AB%98%E9%9B%84%E5%9C%B0%E6%96%B9%E6%B3%95%E9%99%A2_%E6%B2%B3%E6%9D%B1%E8%B7%AF_20110506.jpg/250px-%E8%87%BA%E7%81%A3%E9%AB%98%E9%9B%84%E5%9C%B0%E6%96%B9%E6%B3%95%E9%99%A2_%E6%B2%B3%E6%9D%B1%E8%B7%AF_20110506.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n### Subdivisions\n\nKaohsiung is divided into 38 [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_(Taiwan) \"District (Taiwan)\"), three of which are mountain indigenous districts.[[45]](#cite_note-49)[[46]](#cite_note-50)[[47]](#cite_note-51)[[48]](#cite_note-52) There are a total of 651 [villages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Village_(Taiwan) \"Village (Taiwan)\") in which each village is subdivided into [neighborhoods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neighborhoods \"Neighborhoods\") (鄰). There are 18,584 neighborhoods in Kaohsiung City. [Lingya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingya_District \"Lingya District\") and [Fongshan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fongshan_District \"Fongshan District\") districts are the administrative centers of the city while Lingya and Sinsing Districts are the two most densely populated districts of the city. Kaohsiung has the most numbers of districts among other [special municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_municipality_(Taiwan) \"Special municipality (Taiwan)\") in Taiwan.\n\n|  |  | District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neimen_District \"Neimen District\") | 內門區 | 13,258 | 95.62 | 148.34 | 15,951 | −2,693 |\n| 64000360 | [Maolin District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoyuan_District,_Kaohsiung \"Taoyuan District, Kaohsiung\") | 桃源區 | 4,249 | 928.98 | 4.58 | 4,817 | −568 |\n| 64000380 | [Namasia District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namasia_District \"Namasia District\") | 那瑪夏區 | 3,163 | 252.99 | 12.44 | 3,401 | −238 |\n\n## Notable people\n\n## Transportation\n\n### Port of Kaohsiung\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Ddm_2004_028_Kaohsiung_Harbor.jpg/250px-Ddm_2004_028_Kaohsiung_Harbor.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nA major port, through which pass most of Taiwan's marine imports and exports, is located in the city but is not managed by the city government. Instead, it is administered by Kaohsiung Port Authority, under the Ministry of Transportation. There is a push for Kaohsiung City to annex the Port of Kaohsiung to facilitate better regional planning.\n\nAlso known as the \"Harbour Capital\" of Taiwan, Kaohsiung has always had a strong link with the ocean and maritime transportation. [Ferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferries \"Ferries\") play a key role in everyday transportation, especially for transportation across the harbor. With five terminals and 23 berths, the *Port of Kaohsiung* is Taiwan's largest [container port](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Container_port \"Container port\") and the 13th largest in the world.[[51]](#cite_note-58) In 2007 the port reached its handling capacity with a record trade volume of 10.2 million [twenty-foot equivalent units](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit \"Twenty-foot equivalent unit\") (TEU).[[52]](#cite_note-LLDCN-59) A new container terminal is under construction, increasing future handling capacity by 2 million [TEU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit \"Twenty-foot equivalent unit\") by 2013.[[52]](#cite_note-LLDCN-59)\n\nKaohsiung is one of the biggest ports in the world for importing [shark fins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shark_finning \"Shark finning\"), sold at high prices in the restaurants and shops of Taiwan and China.[[53]](#cite_note-60) They are brought in from overseas and are placed out to dry in the sun on residential rooftops near the port.\n\n### Kaohsiung International Airport\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/%E9%AB%98%E9%9B%84%E5%9C%8B%E9%9A%9B%E6%A9%9F%E5%A0%B4.JPG/250px-%E9%AB%98%E9%9B%84%E5%9C%8B%E9%9A%9B%E6%A9%9F%E5%A0%B4.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nKaohsiung City is also home to Taiwan's second-largest international airport, the [Kaohsiung International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_International_Airport \"Kaohsiung International Airport\"), located in [Siaogang District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siaogang_District \"Siaogang District\") near the city's center. It is one of the three major international airports of Taiwan, serving passengers of the entire southern and southeastern part of the country. However, the size of the airport is relatively small, with short runways compared to other major airports of Taiwan due to its age and its location near the city center, making it impossible for large aircraft such as the [Airbus A380](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airbus_A380 \"Airbus A380\") to land at the airport. As a result, plans for work to begin on a new terminal is expected to start in 2023, and could handle up to 16.5 million passengers once complete.[[54]](#cite_note-61)\n\n### Rapid transit\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Kaohsiung_MRT_Train_at_World_Games_Station.jpg/250px-Kaohsiung_MRT_Train_at_World_Games_Station.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/KMRT_CAF_train_2016-08-27.jpg/250px-KMRT_CAF_train_2016-08-27.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n[Kaohsiung Mass Rapid Transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Mass_Rapid_Transit \"Kaohsiung Mass Rapid Transit\") opened for service in March 2008. The MRT is made up of two lines with 37 stations covering a distance of 42.7 km (26.5 mi).[[55]](#cite_note-62)\n\nTwo of Kaohsiung's MRT stations, [Formosa Boulevard Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formosa_Boulevard_Station \"Formosa Boulevard Station\") and [Central Park Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Park_metro_station_(Taiwan) \"Central Park metro station (Taiwan)\"), were ranked among the top 50 most beautiful subway systems in the world by Metrobits.org in 2011.[[56]](#cite_note-63) In 2012, the two stations respectively are ranked as the 2nd and the 4th among the top 15 most beautiful subway stops in the world by BootsnAll.[[57]](#cite_note-64)\n\n### Circular Light Rail\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d6/Kaohsiung-skyline-2018.jpg/250px-Kaohsiung-skyline-2018.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2a/THSR_Zuoying_Station_and_TRA_New_Zuoying_Station_20080712.jpg/250px-THSR_Zuoying_Station_and_TRA_New_Zuoying_Station_20080712.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nThe [Circular Light Rail Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circular_light_rail \"Circular light rail\") (also known as the Kaohsiung LRT, Kaohsiung Tram) for Kaohsiung City is a [light rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_rail \"Light rail\") line. Construction of Phase 1, known as the Waterside Light Rail began in June 2013 and is in full operation since September 2017. To combat air pollution, usage of the light rail, as well as buses, was made free of charge for electronic ticket holders from December to February, when air pollution is at its peak.[[58]](#cite_note-65)\n\n### Railway\n\nThe city is served by the [Taiwan Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Railway \"Taiwan Railway\")'s [Western Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_line_(Taiwan) \"West Coast line (Taiwan)\") and [Pingtung Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pingtung_Line \"Pingtung Line\"). [Kaohsiung Main Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Main_Station \"Kaohsiung Main Station\") is an underground station, replacing the old ground level station.[[59]](#cite_note-66)\n\n### High Speed Railway\n\nSince 2007, [Taiwan High Speed Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_High_Speed_Rail \"Taiwan High Speed Rail\") has served Kaohsiung via [Zuoying HSR station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zuoying_HSR_station \"Zuoying HSR station\").[[60]](#cite_note-67) On January 4, 2023, the Pingtung HSR extension was approved by Premier Su Zhenchang, and the High Speed Rail extension was confirmed to bypass central Kaohsiung City and [Kaohsiung Main Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Main_Station \"Kaohsiung Main Station\").[[61]](#cite_note-68)\n\n## Sports\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5f/World_Game_2009_Stadium_completed.jpg/250px-World_Game_2009_Stadium_completed.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nKaohsiung is home to Taiwan's largest international-class stadium, the [National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Stadium_(Kaohsiung) \"National Stadium (Kaohsiung)\"), with a maximum capacity of 55,000 seats, as well as [Kaohsiung Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Arena \"Kaohsiung Arena\"). The city hosted the [2009 World Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2009_World_Games \"2009 World Games\") at the National Stadium. Nearly 6,000 athletes, officials, coaches, referees and others from 103 countries participated in the 2009 Kaohsiung World Games.\n\n[TSG Hawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TSG_Hawks \"TSG Hawks\") of Taiwan's [Chinese Professional Baseball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Professional_Baseball_League \"Chinese Professional Baseball League\"), are based in [Chengcing Lake Baseball Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chengcing_Lake_Baseball_Stadium \"Chengcing Lake Baseball Stadium\").[[62]](#cite_note-69)\n\nKaohsiung also has two professional basketball teams, the [Kaohsiung Aquas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Aquas \"Kaohsiung Aquas\") of the [Taiwan Professional Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Professional_Basketball_League \"Taiwan Professional Basketball League\")[[63]](#cite_note-70) and the [Kaohsiung Steelers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Steelers \"Kaohsiung Steelers\") of the [P. League+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P._League+ \"P. League+\").[[64]](#cite_note-71) Kaohsiung was also home to the [Kaohsiung Truth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Truth \"Kaohsiung Truth\") of the [ASEAN Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ASEAN_Basketball_League \"ASEAN Basketball League\"). They were the first team in the history of the league that was based outside Southeast Asia. The team folded in 2017.\n\nThe [TSG SkyHawks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TSG_SkyHawks?action=edit&redlink=1 \"TSG SkyHawks (page does not exist)\") [[zh]](https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-tw/%E5%8F%B0%E9%8B%BC%E5%A4%A9%E9%B7%B9) of the [Taiwan Professional Volleyball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiwan_Professional_Volleyball_League \"Taiwan Professional Volleyball League\") are based in [Fengshan Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fengshan_Arena \"Fengshan Arena\").[[65]](#cite_note-72)\n\nOther recent major sporting events held by Kaohsiung include:\n\n## Education\n\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b8/NSYSU_campus_day03.JPG/250px-NSYSU_campus_day03.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/KSHS.JPG/250px-KSHS.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/%E9%99%B8%E8%BB%8D%E8%BB%8D%E5%AE%98%E5%AD%B8%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%A7%E9%96%80.jpg/250px-%E9%99%B8%E8%BB%8D%E8%BB%8D%E5%AE%98%E5%AD%B8%E6%A0%A1%E5%A4%A7%E9%96%80.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/75/Republic_of_China_Air_Force_Academy_Main_Gate_Front_20111015b.jpg/250px-Republic_of_China_Air_Force_Academy_Main_Gate_Front_20111015b.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\nKaohsiung has a number of colleges and junior colleges offering training in commerce, education, maritime technology, medicine, modern languages, nursing, and technology, as well as various [international schools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_schools \"International schools\") and eight national military schools, including the three major military academies of the country, the [Republic of China Military Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Military_Academy \"Republic of China Military Academy\"), [Republic of China Naval Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Naval_Academy \"Republic of China Naval Academy\") and [Republic of China Air Force Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republic_of_China_Air_Force_Academy \"Republic of China Air Force Academy\").\n\n**Universities**\n\n**Technical and vocational universities**\n\n**High schools and junior high schools**\n\n**International schools**\n\n**Military schools**\n\n(Note: The lists above are not comprehensive.)\n\n## Conferences and events\n\nThe [Kaohsiung Exhibition Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_Exhibition_Center \"Kaohsiung Exhibition Center\"), built by the [Kaohsiung City Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaohsiung_City_Government \"Kaohsiung City Government\"), was opened on 14 April 2014. It includes an exhibition space for 1,500 booths, and a convention hall for 2,000 people.\n\nThe center hosted the Taiwan International Boat Show in May 2014.[[66]](#cite_note-73) Another conference and event-related venue is the newly renovated [International Convention Center Kaohsiung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Convention_Center_Kaohsiung \"International Convention Center Kaohsiung\") in 2013.\n\n## Sister cities and friendship cities\n\nThere are three [Consulates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consulates \"Consulates\") in
